Not fixed (`// Complexity tradeoff:` at `probe`): a disjunct that no point holds still costs one propagation of the whole formula, so cross from k = 51 to 99 still costs about k (k - 50) (2.0, 15.1 and 52.8 G at k = 52, 72 and 96; was 22.9 G at 52): `chain_cross_above_its_width_scales` (12 to 96) under `#[ignore]`, ledger LIN-probe-refute; a cheaper proof needs a propagation that reports which disjuncts it refutes, or undoable propagation. cross_dead 18.18 G at k = 256 (32 to 256: 17.3 times); the growth left is outside `probe` (9 settles per call at every k), as on cross_live; its place is not measured. |
| T6-ratlit | done | The checker's literal type of a `rat` no longer builds the literal's digits (COMPLEXITY.md K30). It hashes a `rat` or big `int` by its residues modulo 2^61 - 1 (`Rat::residues`), and compares, orders and tests membership on the exact value, so two literals of equal residues are two types (`literals_of_equal_residues_are_different_types`). A type prints a decimal `rat` as the one literal `meKr`, `m` an integer not divisible by 10 (`Rat::literal_text`), the form the type syntax writes (`SignedLit`): `1e400000r`, `1e-400000r`, `15e-4r` for `1.5e-3r`, `5e-1r` for `0.5r`; equal literals written differently print alike, and the ZIR type text reads the form back. Tests `l01_22_type_representation::rat_literals` (8: equal forms, order against rat and int literals, printing and reading back, 400 random literals against their built form, a computed power, equal residues, and two scaling tests: N = 2^20 and 2^23, 1.0 times the instructions; N of 200 and 1600 digits, 1.5 times). Negative control with the old `to_string` print and hash (not committed): 8.0 times the bytes at N = 2^20 and 2^23, limit 3, fails. Complexity tradeoff at `Rat::literal_text`: the part that is not a power of 10 is printed in full (a literal's own digits; a computed constant's digits), and a computed quotient that is not a decimal prints as `1r/3r`, which the type syntax cannot write. The checker folds no arithmetic into a literal type, so no other operation needs the value. |
| S22e-quad | done | COMPLEXITY.md S22e-quad. The split of S22e ran the backward transfer over the whole chain once per class, O(k²) for `k` distinct remainders (58.0 times the instructions from 32 to 256). Now the classes keep what they compute without reading the class (`Kept`), an `if` on the remainder is read from one summary over every class (`Tx::by_class`, a persistent segment tree over class indices with lazy affine tags), and the split over the whole box is tried before the first transfer, which is skipped when the split is exact: O(k log k), 8.5 times from 32 to 256 (`many_remainders_scales`). Repair 1: that early split is given up after at most one class, and the transfer runs first, when the box holds `x` within two periods of the modulus or when a class's run reads a remainder guard itself (`min` arms, boolean arms); run first there it was quadratic where main was linear (45.3, 45.7 and 64.9 times for 8 times the size; `remainders_read_by_each_class_keep_the_transfer_first_scales` and `remainders_read_by_each_class_keep_their_preimages`). Open, the same on main: terms the summary does not read over many periods (a `min` chain about k^2.7, tests above the chain k^2) and the inexact linear stage after a split given up for a mixed stretch (C17); ledger S22e-wide. Tests in `l01_25_preimage_catalog::s22`: `remainder_chains_of_every_shape_match_the_interpreter` (k = 0, 1, 2, repeated remainders, negative moduli, a guard between the remainder guards, affine arms) and `remainder_chains_outside_the_shared_summary_keep_their_preimages` (`min` arms, remainders and moduli at the ends of `i64`, domains at both ends), each point checked against the interpreter. `preimage_ref` comparison unchanged; two cases at `i64::MIN` now exact and empty without listing the box. |
| S22e-wide | done | COMPLEXITY.md S22e-wide. (A) a term that reads no remainder is summarized once over the box and the summary used in every class (`Tx::by_class`): the `min` chain 7.8 times and the tests above the chain 8.3 times for 8 times the size (main about k^2.7 and k^2), both exact. (B) a split over many periods no longer runs the linear stage, whose translation gave each `x mod m` its own auxiliary pair; the split's own relation is projected over the box's hull and checked exact by a sweep (`covers`, O(P log P)), and an early answer lets the transfer and the later split run first: `!=` chain 1.2 times and `many_remainders` below `i mod 1024 == 0` 5.8 times (main 0.86G to 3.0G and 11.3G to 84.1G), both exact. Changed cases against `store::preimage_ref` named in s22.rs and COMPLEXITY.md. Tests in `l01_25_preimage_catalog::s22`: `remainders_over_many_periods_scale`, `remainders_over_many_periods_keep_their_preimages`, `remainder_splits_at_their_corners_keep_their_preimages`. Open: terms the summary still does not read, and the merged middle periods over many periods (recorded at `split_residues`). |
| T6-bound-load | done | Three flaws left by T6 (9a413d5). (1) The `L .. U -> V` bound check ran in elaboration only, so a parameter's annotation was never checked on some paths. Now `Tables` lists every written type once (parameters, lambda parameters, defaults, tests, `is`), and `check::check_with`, which every path calls, checks their bounds in one pass (`Ck::bounds`, `elab::kernel_type`). Load, check and batch give the same message (`l01_23_subtyping::bound_paths`, `l01_29_diagnostics::batch`). Elaboration no longer stops at a failed bound, so `x is (#a .. #b -> int)` now also reports that `is` cannot test a function type. (2) A rejection whose check the kernel refused now names the cause with `Ty::refused`: the overload message "does not take", in both its forms (no arrow meets the argument, `refused_among`; no arrow is left), the literal failure and the need-result check (`check_on`). A decided failure is unchanged: an arrow that some argument fails by a decided check gives no cause (`refusals::an_overload_rejected_by_a_decided_check_names_no_cause`). The kernel's value builder (`types/empty.rs` `find_rep`) built no value for a cofinite set of rationals or floats, so `f64 <: int` and `f64 <: rat` were `Refused(NoWitness)`, and `x + f64(1)` with `x = 1r` would have reported that cause; each carrier now gives one of its first values not listed (`refusals::a_cofinite_set_of_rationals_or_floats_has_a_value`). Both tests fail on 1c40ccc. Refused inputs cannot be reached from source: the checker's `Ty` drops a written difference of tuples (`((triple) \ (boxes),)` is shown as `((triple),)`), and operator and prelude arrows take carriers. So the refused cases are tested through `check::operator` with kernel types: the seven-box cover (`TupleSplit`) and a morphism (`NoWitness`) on `neg`, `+`, `<` and `!` (`refusals::an_overload_whose_check_was_refused_says_why`). The decided cases are tested end to end. A literal against a tuple or morphism part is always decided, because a scalar never meets one. The default check with a class goes through `flow`, which uses carrier masks and so is always decided. (3) An untracked declared type prints through the kernel printer (`check::declared_text`), and the text parses back as the same type (`l01_29_diagnostics::untracked`). No source program reaches that message: `denoted` gives the unknown only for an empty `&`, which the grammar does not produce, and `_` in a type is layer L10. Scaling: 250 and 2,000 bounded parameters through `load`, counted in instructions by `scaling::check_order` (`bound_paths::the_bound_walk_scales_linearly`, within the limit of 24 times). The negative control, a quadratic walk that was not committed, did 60.2 times the instructions (2.20e9 to 1.32e11) and fails it. Not done (follow-up T6-ports): `Tables::written` leaves out output-port types, so `f() -> (out: #a .. #b -> int) { 0 }` with `main() = 0;` is accepted; more broadly the port types of a function other than `main` are not checked at all (`f() -> (out: int) { 0 }` is accepted; only main's ports are checked, `run.rs`). |
| T6-ports | done | Two holes left by T6-bound-load. (1) `Tables::written` left out output-port types, so the bound of a port type was never checked: `main() -> (out: #a .. #b -> int)` gave the input-port message of `run::program`, never the bound error; each port's type now joins `written` in the order of the text (`elab::Tables::collect_stmt`), and load, check and batch report the bound (`l01_23_subtyping::bound_paths::a_port_whose_morphism_bound_fails_is_rejected`). (2) The ports of a function other than `main` were not checked at all: `f() -> (out: #a .. #b -> int) { 0 }` with `main() = 0;` and `log(positional s) -> (out: Files)` were accepted. `resolve` now rejects such a port, at the port, with "output port `out` of `f` is `Lines` at layer L1" (DESIGN.md step L1.19: at L1 an output is `Lines`); the ports of the unit's own `main` stay with `run::program`, which checks them with its inputs, so the messages of a program's ports are unchanged; a `main` nested in a block is no program's `main`, and its ports are checked as a helper's (`g() = { main() -> (out: Files) { 0 } 1 };` is rejected at 2:14) (`l01_19_whole_file_ports::files::a_helper_port_is_lines_at_l1`). `run::program` gave an output port of `main` of a type that is no port (`int`) the input-port message "is `Lines`, `Files` or `CommandLine`"; it now says "output port ... is `Lines`" (`files::ports_are_lines_or_files_at_l1`). All three tests fail on main a9330ae. Scaling: COMPLEXITY K45. |
| TD-ends | done | Interval ends of any size, replacing the `i64` end rule (docs/thresholds.md replacement 10; tradeoffs review finding 4). `End::Fin(Num)`: `store::int::Num` is one machine word while the value fits in `i64` and a big integer past it, and a value has one form. `End::from_i128`, `interval::rounded` and the outward rounding are gone; sums, products, offsets and `div` are exact across the range in both directions. `presburger::hull_from` decides each unbounded side by the recession cone (Meyer 1974) and finds a bound by doubling then bisection over `Int`, so a bound past `i64` is exact, not "no bound". `Affine::plus` and `Bounds::offset` are exact. `Landmarks` hold `Num`; a `Value::Big` literal is a landmark and a point end in the preimage. A string longer than `i64::MAX` has its exact length as its interval. The canonical serialization writes a big end as tag 3 and its decimal text. Tests changed to assert the exact result: `entries::bounds_past_i64_keep_the_box` (the last case is `x [0..=1]; y [-2..=2]; necessary`, since the points that hold are not a box), `omega::a_bound_past_i64_is_exact` (was `a_bound_past_i64_is_searched_within_i64`), `bounds::an_offset_moves_every_member` (round trips by `i64::MAX` and `i64::MIN`), `cell::a_string_too_long_for_an_i64_has_its_exact_length`. New: `l01_24_refinement_lattices::ends` (4 tests: `i64::MAX`, `i64::MIN`, one past each, sums, products and offsets crossing the boundary both ways, a bound of `2^200`), `entries::a_literal_of_2_to_the_200_is_an_exact_end`, and scaling tests `ends_in_one_word_scale_linearly` and `ends_of_2_to_the_200_scale_linearly` (COMPLEXITY.md S33). Negative control, not committed: all `Num` arithmetic through the big integer made the one-word work 1.35 to 1.46 times larger. Repair 1: a power outside `i128` is kept as base and exponent (`Num::pow`, `Word::Pow`), so the bound of `x ** 10^8` is built and compared in under a second (main gave +inf; the first TD-ends version wrote out 10^8 bits, k = 10^8 past 56 s); `Num` equality and hashing go by value (hash by residue modulo 2^61 - 1); `IntervalSet` merges adjacent pieces by `End::at_most_succ`, which does not write a power out. Tests: `ends::the_bound_of_a_large_power_is_built_and_compared_without_its_digits`, `ends::a_kept_power_equals_the_same_value_written_out`, `ends::kept_powers_order_and_hash_as_their_values_written_out`, `scaling::power_bounds_scale_with_the_bits_of_the_exponent` (1.1 times from k = 10^6 to 8 * 10^6; the control with powers written out, not committed, 20.8 times). `Bounds::normal` splits its borrow by field instead of copying the hull end. Repair 2: a value outside `i128` built from a power is kept as `(c * core + d) / den` (`store::int::Kept`; the core a power or a power of a floor root of a kept power), so `x ** k + 1`, `k * x ** k`, `x ** k - 1`, `div`, `mod` and gcd by a value that is not kept, and the floor and ceiling roots of a power keep the form (the verifier measured 14 s for `x ** k + 1` at k = 10^8, 17 s for the preimage of `x ** 100000000 + 1 >= 50`, and past 60 s for the cube root of 2^(10^8) and the preimage of `x ** 3 <= 2 ** 100000000`; now each is under 4 ms). Ordering compares one core by the exact sign of a linear form, a root against the power it came from without its digits, and others at doubling precision; values outside `i64` hash by their sign. Tests: `ends::kept_forms_compute_as_their_values_written_out` (156 values in kept forms against `BigInt`: order, equality, hash, sums, differences, products, `div`, `mod`, gcd, powers and roots), `ends::arithmetic_and_roots_on_a_large_power_keep_its_form` (k = 10^8, under 1 s), `l01_25_preimage_catalog::kept_ends` (2 tests, the preimages above under 1 s), `scaling::kept_ends_scale_linearly` (8.1 times from n = 200 to 1600) and `scaling::kept_arithmetic_scales_with_the_bits_of_the_exponent` (1.1 times from k = 10^6 + 1 to 8 times it); negative control with the kept-plus-scalar sum written out, not committed: 27.1 times, k = 10^8 past 60 s. Repair 3: a kept value is a sum of terms, `(c_1 * core_1 + ... + c_n * core_n + d) / den`, each core a product of powers of integers and of floor roots; terms are collected by core and the powers of one integer folded (`fold`), so `3 ** k + 5 ** k`, `3 ** (k + 1) - 3 ** k` (exactly `2 * 3 ** k`), `3 ** k * 5 ** (k - 1)` and `(2 ** k + 1) ** 3` keep their form (the verifier measured past 60 s and past 30 s for `zedc test` of `x ** (3 ** 100000000 + 5 ** 100000000)` and `x ** (3 ** 100000001 - 3 ** 100000000)`; now 0.01 s and 0.03 s). The approximation of a kept root is kept with its atom, so the 186-value test runs in 14.6 s of processor time (12.1 s for 156 values before). Values inside `i128` hash as themselves. Every `// Complexity tradeoff:` comment in `store/int.rs` names what it gives up, why, its trigger, its test and row S33, and a complete solution. Tests: `ends::sums_of_unrelated_large_powers_keep_their_form` (k = 10^8, under 1 s), `ends::kept_values_with_no_kept_form_are_written_out` (the written-out cases, exact), 24 more values in `kept_forms_compute_as_their_values_written_out`, and the kept scaling test extended (8.69M to 9.59M instructions, 1.1 times); negative control with sums of two kept values of different first cores written out, not committed: the scaling test and the k = 10^8 sums test past 60 s. Repair 4: each integer base is reduced to its least root (`least_root`), and two cores that differ in form are compared over a coprime base of the integers on which they differ (`Core::same`), so `x ** (3 ** e * 9 ** (e + 1) - 3 ** (3e + 2))` no longer writes out its digits (30.6 s at e = 10^6 before; `ends::equal_cores_over_bases_that_share_factors_cancel_without_their_digits` under 1 s at e = 10^6 and 8 * 10^6, `scaling::equal_cores_scale_with_the_bits_of_the_exponent`, 736527 to 826527 instructions, 1.1 times; negative control with both off, not committed: past 60 s). The hash stays by value inside `i128` and by sign outside it, which is consistent with equality; the earlier tradeoff comment proposed hashing roots by sign and big values by residue, which would break that, and is replaced; `ends::equal_values_in_different_forms_hash_alike` tests a big value equal to a kept root and kept forms equal to the values written out. Repair 5: rebased onto main 66945e0 as one commit; main's S22 transfer, the reference transfer `preimage_ref` and the S22, overflow, constant-minus and powers tests ported to exact ends (the rounding flag is gone; the S22a summary gives up on an end at or past an end of `i64`). repair 4's least root tried an integer root of all the base's bits for each prime index, cubic in the bits (verify#5: the cube of an end of 2^32768 + 3 took 69 s, 3 to 19 µs on repair 3); it now takes a 2-adic root of bits / p bits per odd prime p and checks bit count and three residues before any full power, so the cube takes 9.7 ms at 32768 bits and 89 ms at 262144. The coprime base and `exponents_over` take every power of a shared factor out at once (O(log k) divisions for multiplicity k), so `(2^n * 3) ** 1000 == 2 ** (1000 n) * 3 ** 1000` takes 0.15 ms at n = 8192 (135 ms before). The negation of the kept value 2^127 is now the big value `i128::MIN`, so it hashes as its value and `to_i128` returns it (verify#5 found the two hashes differ; the ledger item KEPT-neg-hash). Tests: `scaling::the_cube_of_a_wide_end_scales_with_its_bits`, `scaling::equality_over_a_wide_base_scales_with_its_bits`, `ends::a_wide_end_is_kept_as_a_power_of_its_least_root`, the pair `(-(2^127 kept), Big(-2^127))` in `ends::equal_values_in_different_forms_hash_alike`, and a unit test of `least_root` against trial for every base below 50000 and powers of 127-bit bases to indices up to 210; negative controls, not committed: repair 4's least root 820 times the work for 8 times the bits, the one-factor-per-step coprime base 34.5 times. Repair 6 (on main 66945e0): the wide-base equality test runs at 65536 and 524288 bits, past the size at which num-bigint changes to Burnikel-Ziegler division, which gave 26.3 times the bytes from 8192 to 65536 bits under the byte check from SCALE-bytes (17.2 times now; the one-factor-per-step control 63.9 times). With exact ends, four l01_25 expectations that main rounded or listed are now exact and not listed: `overflow::an_arm_or_a_second_input_past_max` (`y [-10..=2, 4..=10]`), both `constant_minus` tests at the ends of `i64`, and `t3_gaps` past `k = 37` in s22. `kept_ends` adds a domain unbounded on both sides, which the summary reads with the ends of `i64` standing for the infinities. The l01_39 nest of 256 functions now runs on a 256 MiB thread: the preimage still recurses on the term (T2-mid), and its frames grew about 7% (4.1 KB a level against 3.8 KB on main), past the 2 MiB of a test thread. Printing a kept value, and the other cases COMPLEXITY.md S33 lists, still write out its digits. Still `i64` after TD-ends: `Congruence` (modulus and residue), `Affine::times` (`None` past `i64`) and the end of `x ** k` past `u32`; TD-cong made them exact. |
| F44-memo | done | `zed_tests::scaling` runs the warm-up and every measured run on a new thread (`alone`, by `on_a_thread` with the stack the caller has left, read by `stacker`), so zed-kernel's per-thread memos start empty in each run and the run's bytes allocated (SCALE-bytes) are added to the measuring thread's count; a larger run whose thread counts 0 bytes is refused with its own message, so a lost count cannot pass (unit tests that allocate nothing use `check_instructions_only`, as do m0_09 `deep_scaling` "copy and free" and "the key", whose runs are `Arc` copies that allocate nothing and on main compared 0 bytes with 0); unit test `each_run_starts_with_empty_thread_memos` fails when `alone` runs on the caller's thread (1 fresh run of 5, negative control, not committed), and `a_run_alone_counts_the_bytes_its_thread_allocates` when `alone` drops its thread's bytes (0 against 16352); closures are `Fn + Sync`, so tests that held `Cell` or `RefCell` hold atomics or a `Mutex` (l01_22 with `deep_types.rs`, l01_23, l01_36, l25_08, m0_09). `l01_21` builds each size's front end before the timing and runs on a copy. Fresh ratios: l01_10 7.7, l01_21 passes; `l01_23::scaling::the_parts_scale` fails at 48.0 (12.7 M against 610 M; the old harness passes it at 7.0, negative control, not committed). Cause, by perf and a phase probe: `Part::forced` on an expression is O(atoms · size) (`is_empty` 11.0 M, 597.8 M, 36.6 G at 16, 128, 1024). T6's one-pass `forced` (merged 5b78cbd) fixes it: rebased on it, the test runs again and passes (3.67 M, 28.6 M, 229.7 M on the phase probe); `x13.rs` and the harness's own unit test now hold atomics and a `Mutex`. The gap this left, a bare thread started inside a run losing its bytes, is closed by SCALE-threads. COMPLEXITY.md F44, ledger F44-forced |
| SCALE-threads | done | The bytes a scaling run allocates now count every thread the run starts, however it starts it (bare `std::thread::spawn`, scoped, nested), as the instruction counter with `inherit` already did. Choice: per-thread counts that a new thread takes from its parent, not one count for the process, because the tests of one binary run beside each other and a process count mixes their bytes. `zed-alloc-count` defines its own `pthread_create` (std starts every thread by it; it calls the C library's, found by `dlsym(RTLD_NEXT)`), which gives the new thread an `Arc<Count>` holding its parent's; an allocation adds to its thread's count and each ancestor's (the chain is as deep as the nesting of thread starts). The main thread was not started that way, so it counts alone and the test harness's threads count on their own. `zed_alloc_count::add` is gone, since a parent sees its children's bytes as they allocate; `on_a_thread` and `alone` no longer add. Tests: `zed-alloc-count` `a_thread_counts_in_the_count_of_the_thread_that_started_it` (grandchild 4096 bytes seen in the child), `other_threads_do_not_change_this_threads_count` (measured on a sibling of 4 allocating threads), `a_thread_started_by_a_direct_call_of_pthread_create_is_counted`; `scaling` `the_bytes_of_a_bare_thread_a_run_starts_count` (on the calling thread exactly 16352 bytes, as before; on a bare thread, and on a thread that one starts, at least that; n² bytes on a bare thread fail the check). Repair 1: std records each new thread in one map of the live threads for the process (`stack_overflow/thread_info.rs`), on the new thread, and that insertion allocates 0 or about 1 KiB depending on the other threads alive, so a count holding a thread start is not deterministic beside other tests (verify 1: 16488 bytes alone, 17672 beside 8 threads; the unit test comparing a nested run with a joined one failed in 1 of 10 runs). `compare_by` now compares the bytes `alone` counts inside its thread, which hold every thread the run starts but not the start of its own thread, the least of the runs of each size; the unit test asserts only lower bounds. Test `the_bytes_of_a_run_alone_do_not_depend_on_threads_starting_beside_it` (50 runs beside 8 threads that start threads, each exactly 16352); negative control (the meter's bytes around `alone`, as before the repair, not committed): 16608 in 44 runs, 17152 in 6. The zed-alloc-count doc names this and that a test libtest runs on the main thread (when it cannot start one) does not count the threads it starts. Negative control (main's allocator with the new `scaling.rs`, not committed): that test fails at `joined >= 16352`, with 3 others that relied on `add`. Full suite with a temporary ratio log on both trees (not committed), base b0a733ff against the branch after repair 1: 330 pieces each, 0 failures; 166 of 782 bytes ratios changed, nearly all because both sizes count 232 bytes fewer (base counted the start of `alone`'s thread on the measuring thread; now `n² bytes` compares exactly 1000000 and 64000000); the largest rise is 0.12 (`printing powers` 7.96 to 8.07, `functions each calling the one before twice, alike` at 6 and 48 7.55 to 7.67); the few runs whose bytes moved by more than 232 (`the nth union built`, `a meet of cycles`, `lowering n functions each called once in place`) vary by as much between runs of one tree (three reruns); no test now fails. Gap notes removed from `scaling.rs` |
| PERF-portable | done | The scaling tests measure work on macOS and Windows, not only Linux. Linux is unchanged: user-mode instructions by `perf_event_open` (inheriting threads), else the task clock, else its own error. macOS: `proc_pid_rusage(getpid(), RUSAGE_INFO_V4)` `ri_instructions`, and `ri_cycles` when the instructions read 0, the message naming which (`unit`). Windows: `QueryProcessCycleTime` cycles. Cycles are inexact, so they get the task clock's repeats (5 runs, 3 measurements). Both counters are of the whole process, not of a thread and the threads it starts, and off Linux the bytes allocated are one count for the process, so on macOS and Windows `Meter::new` stops with its own error unless libtest runs one test at a time (`--test-threads=1`, `--test-threads 1` or `RUST_TEST_THREADS=1`). The calls are in `zed_alloc_count::os` (the workspace forbids `unsafe_code`), each behind `#[cfg(target_os)]`, with the Apple and Microsoft documentation cited; `windows-sys` 0.61.2 was already in Cargo.lock. The macOS and Windows paths are compiled and not run: `cargo check` and `cargo clippy -D warnings` pass for x86_64-apple-darwin, aarch64-apple-darwin and x86_64-pc-windows-msvc on zed-alloc-count (all targets) and on `scaling.rs` with its tests in a temporary crate (no rustup here: `RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP=1 -Zbuild-std` on Fedora's rust-src, clang and llvm-ar). The full `zed-tests` crate does not build off Linux, before this change as after: `zed-rt/src/stack.rs` uses `libc::sysinfo`, `MAP_STACK` and `mmap` (ledger PORTABLE-rt). Tests: `a_meter_takes_the_first_counter_that_moves` (zero then moving counter gives the second, with its unit and repeats; none moving gives none), `a_meter_measures_by_any_counter_it_is_given` (linear passes, quadratic fails naming 64.0 times), `one_test_at_a_time_is_read_from_the_options_of_libtest_or_its_variable`; tests of per-thread bytes and of the task clock are Linux-only. | TYPE-forms | merged, unpushed (6c2c0510, acc4f3ae, e3b9b5c6, 0dce3ee0, b6d0a2ee, rebased on main 34c570dd, and the commit that records this) | The bounds clause `t for X in U` (DESIGN.md sec:grammar `TypeBounds`, binding loosest) in the AST (`TypeKind::For`, `TypeBound`), resolver, elaborator, formatter and checker. Each bounded variable is bounded on the outermost signature of its clause whose arrows name it, the one a call binds it in: resolve requires every use to lie in one outermost function type of the clause, and `check::Denote` reads the type from the top down, each use recording its bound on that function type, so the cost is linear however deeply clauses nest. Diagnostics, each with its span and a note: an unbounded name is a type variable of no clause (as before), a bound on a variable the type does not hold, a variable bounded twice (in one clause or in two), a bound that is not a union of carriers, a variable used outside a function type or in two function types of its clause, and an `is` test of a bounded type (elab: a test has no call to choose a carrier). The formatter writes the clause at level 0, below arrows; the checker prints the clause after a bounded signature and `check::read::parse_type` reads printed types through the AST. Six grammar cases (393 cases in all). An unbounded name inside a clause gets a note naming the innermost clause. Passing a bounded function where a function type is expected (`ty::fits_sig`): the expected type's bounded variables are renamed apart and held rigid, each standing for every carrier of its bound, so the result does not depend on variable names and two types that use one name are not confused; a function of one carrier, `(x: int) -> int`, is the function `(x: T) -> T for T in int`. A bounded variable that no parameter holds is fixed by no argument, so it stands for the union of its bound's carriers at a call (`close`), in a fit of a passed function on both sides (`fits_in`: such a variable of the expected type is not held rigid, and one of the passed function stays unbound after its parameters), and in the check of a lambda's result (`fit_lambda`); before repair 1, `() -> T for T in int \| f64` did not fit itself and `() -> 1` was refused there. The type printer is linear (COMPLEXITY.md F66; it was quadratic in depth on main). Tests `l01_26_type_resolution::bounds` (26): parse, round trip, accepted and refused calls, one carrier, two variables, nested clauses, printed types read back, the one-tuple `(T,)` and `L .. U -> V` read by the checker, a wider bound passed where a narrower one is expected (accepted) and the reverse (refused), renamed equal bounds, a one-carrier function passed as bounded, a result-only variable (passed as itself, as a narrower function, as a lambda, and refused outside its bound or with a wider one), and functions whose parameters are functions. Only five carriers exist, so scaling is measured in the number of bounded variables and of nested clauses, not of carriers in one bound. Scaling (COMPLEXITY.md F65): `many_bounded_variables_scale`, 800 and 6,400 bounds, 8.0 times; old per-variable argument scan in `apply_one` and `refuses` (from main) 28.3 times, fixed; `nested_bounds_clauses_scale`, 400 and 3,200 nested clauses, passes; the old walk of each clause's whole type 36.0 times (26.6 at 200 and 1,600, new code 8.9). Negative controls run once, not committed. `passing_a_function_of_many_bounded_variables_scales`, 800 and 6,400, 8.3 times (linear lookup control 59.8); `printing_a_deep_function_type_scales` 7.9 times (old printer 30.5). Repair 3: `fits_in` no longer walks each signature's parameters or rewrites the expected signature at each depth; `Sig::held` is set when the signature is built and each side is read through a persistent map (`ty::Env`), so passing a function type nested d deep costs O(d) (COMPLEXITY F65: 8.1 and 8.2 times at 400 and 3,200 deep, plain and bounded; 26.6 and 59.0 times before). Not done: a bound on a declaration's own parameter types (`f(x: T for T in int) = x` is refused as standing outside a function type); a passed function whose own bounded variable occurs only under a function-typed parameter (`((x: U) -> U) -> U for U in int \| f64` where the same type with `T` is expected) is refused, soundly but needlessly, since `fits_sig` binds no variable of the outer function inside a nested function type; `Denote` does not record a bound on a variable that occurs only in a range's lower end, so that the printed type reads back; an inferred bound with the bit for values of no carrier prints as `for T in int \| other values` (`ty::mask_text`, from main), which is not user-writable syntax; the printer writes a nested bounded parameter with doubled parentheses, `((((x: T) -> T for T in int)) -> U for U in bool)`, which reads back but is redundant |
| LIN-probe-refute | merged, unpushed (78e5f708, c2f7eb25, d77c5983, 95f03795, rebased on main 1ee1bb2f, and the commit that records this) | COMPLEXITY.md S22, `presburger::probe` on cross from k = 51 to 99, where it refuted each disjunct no point holds (the bounds `i <= j - 1` below the `i >= 47` the formula implies at k = 96) with its own propagation, about k (k - 50) settles. New l01_38 `probe`: guards beyond the inputs answered against every point (w 1 to 8, k up to 3w), one guard, every disjunct refuted, none refuted, and `guards_beyond_the_inputs_scale` (16 to 128, symbols whose names do not sort in chain order) 11.0 times, 41.7 with the control. The whole `Preimage` equals the one without `learn` on 2,851 cases of all families (cross families up to k = 96). | JIT-reloc | merged and pushed (e408858b, rebased on main 43b7cdb5, and the commit that records this) | `l01_36_cranelift::table::bare_values_made_and_dropped_scale` panicked once under load in cranelift-jit `compiled_blob.rs:172`, `TryFromIntError(PosOverflow)`. That line applies `Reloc::X86PCRel4`, which non-position-independent x86-64 code emits only for the address of a symbol of the module itself: the `lea` in `codegen::Gen::root` that takes each lambda entry's address for `rt_register`. cranelift-jit's `SystemMemoryProvider` maps code one piece at a time, so the root and an entry can lie more than 2 GiB apart when other mappings land between them (inferred: another test thread's `Stack`, as large as memory; 0 of 21 runs of the l01_36 group at load 42 to 63 reproduced it by chance). Fix (`zed_native::run_in`): the JIT builds position-independent code on x86-64, as cranelift-jit's own default does. Every symbol address is then loaded through `X86GOTPCRel4`, which cranelift-jit turns into a `lea` when the symbol is within 2 GiB and otherwise into a load from an entry it adds at the end of the same allocation, and calls out of reach go through jumps added there, so no relocation depends on where the pieces lie. Rejected: one region reserved up front (`ArenaMemoryProvider`), since its size is a cap. Test: the unit test `placement::code_runs_with_its_functions_more_than_2_gib_apart` runs two lambdas, at both opt levels, with a memory provider that puts every allocation in its own 4 GiB slot; on the old flags it panics at `compiled_blob.rs:172:80` with the reported error, and passes with the fix. The 31 scaling checks of l01_36 give the same ratios with and without the change (within 0.1; e.g. n lambdas 8.05 and 8.05). Open: on other hosts the JIT stays non-position-independent (cranelift-jit refuses it there), and AArch64 takes a local function's address within 4 GiB; untested, as only x86-64 is a host now. No COMPLEXITY row: no cost changes |
| JIT-farapart-ge | merged and pushed (14c86f9c, rebased on main 6f3a6239, and the commit that records this) | The `FarApart` memory provider of the unit test `placement::code_runs_with_its_functions_more_than_2_gib_apart` (crates/zed-native/src/lib.rs) refused an allocation only when the slot counter equalled `SLOTS`. The counter is a `fetch_add`, so it keeps rising after the first refusal: the second allocation past the 16 slots got slot 17, passed the check, and `mprotect` was called on an address past the reservation, which another mapping may own (then its protection changes and the provider hands out memory it does not own). Fix: `slot >= SLOTS`. Test: the unit test `placement::far_apart_refuses_every_allocation_past_its_slots` allocates 16 slots and then twice more, and requires both refusals to give the slots message; with `==` the second refusal is `Cannot allocate memory (os error 12)` from `mprotect` and the test fails. A difference flips the sign of its second operand as a flag and asks for the result's width; `mod` by a divisor of one word folds the dividend's words with no copy, and by a wider divisor folds blocks of its width; `div` asks for the dividend's width (16 times for a divisor wider than 64 bits, as repair 7 below sets) before dividing; the kept-power paths ask before each copy. Type errors from primitives and the evaluator now print each operand's type in user-writable syntax (``Lt applied to `int`, `rat` ``, ``a value of type `int` is not a function``, `Value::kind_type`) instead of its digits, so `wide < 0.5` no longer prints 2^30 bits; a bit word outside its domain says "it is negative" or "it is at least 2 ** width". The message cannot give a span: kernel terms carry none. Tests `l01_17_value_representation::in_place` (child tests under the lowered limit, small cases, a scaling test at power 1). COMPLEXITY.md K30 repair 6. Open: `Rat::mul` and `Rat::div` stay unchecked for callers outside the evaluator. Repair 7 (review): the asks were below measured peaks, so wide divisions and a wide `mod` aborted under a limit; `div` by a wide divisor now asks 16 times the dividend (peak 15.0), `rem` 24 times the divisor (peak 21.3), the modular-power step 32 times (peak 25.2), the plain sum, `exact_div` and `aligned` 16 times (peaks 8.8, 10.9, 10.7); refusal and answer tests at margins between the old and new asks, negative control aborts on the old ones; COMPLEXITY.md K30 repair 7. Repair 8 (verify#2): the asks of `rem_built`'s modular-power step, `exact_div` and `aligned` carry Complexity tradeoff comments, and the comments of `rem_built` and `div_euclid` no longer say no input is refused for its size; child tests `a_kept_power_modulo_a_wide_number_is_refused_below_its_ask` (margin 28 times the divisor, between the measured peak and the ask) and `an_exact_quotient_of_a_wide_number_is_refused_when_memory_is_short`; COMPLEXITY.md K30 repair 8. |

| LIT-adopt | merged and pushed (a903df85, 6f85066a, 126c810a, rebased on main 5c0587ea, and the commit that records this; spec 82210ed, b091f0a, b4d27e6) | COMPLEXITY.md F69. Three adoption defects, each fixed where adoption is decided (`check::adopted`, `carriers_at`, `no_one_carrier`). (1) A literal compared by `==` with an inferred parameter, or at an open type variable, followed nothing and stayed int, so `isOne(1.0)` was false: it now follows the class's carrier (`Info::follows`, rewritten by the elaborator to a match on f64 and rat), and a literal that is no value of that carrier is unequal (`reading == 0.5` at int is false). A literal argument at an open type variable keeps its own carrier. (2) An int literal at `f64 \| String` or `f64 \| bool` now adopts f64. (3) Int literals in a tuple adopt the components' carriers: `sum((x, 2))` with x: f64 and `t((1, 2))` at `(f64, f64)`, nested too. DESIGN L1.26 said only that a literal adopts the carrier "an operator or a parameter expects"; at a union with two numeric carriers no one carrier is expected, so the item decides, and the spec sentence (wt/lit-adopt-spec) states: the literal keeps its own carrier (int, or f64 for a decimal) when the type holds it (`1` at `int \| f64` is int, `0.5` at `int \| rat` is rat, its one adoptable carrier) and is rejected otherwise (`1` at `f64 \| rat`: "adopts no one carrier", naming `1.0` and `1r`). The preimage catalog (`Node::Follow`) and the reduced product (`plain_comparison`, `plain_test`) read a following literal; `preimage_ref` has no Follow node and is unchanged (ledger LIT-follow-tuple notes it). `sum` reads its components in written order. Three quadratic paths a deep tuple reached were fixed: the data check on each tuple level (`tuple_step`, `data` by reference), the `seen` map keeping a cloned type per tuple level (a tuple written out keeps none; `Ck::tuple_seen` rebuilds it for a derivation), and `equal` cloning each side (`Side` borrows). Control on base 29d86d1e: deep d=64 0.18 s, d=512 13.8 s; now 15 ms and 128 ms. Tests: `l01_26_type_resolution::literals` (11, with the `zed/readings.zed` unit of 18 rows read as sensor readings, and scaling at n and 8n over width and depth), one each in l01_17 equality, l01_29 derivations and batch (batch and single-file checking agree on the `f64 \| rat` rejection); corpus counts in l01_33/34/36/37 moved by the new rows. Full suite `tools/run_tests.sh 2` (load 24 to 58 on 32 cores): 333 pieces, 2 failed: l01_07_fences, failing on base 29d86d1e too, and l01_29 batch on a placeholder message since filled in and rerun. Follow-up LIT-follow-tuple: a literal inside a tuple compared with a class component does not follow the class's carrier. Repair #1 (verifier's defects): (a) the reduced product read a following comparison by forwarding the compared value again per test, 2^d on nested following comparisons (6.19 s at d = 12); now the value is found once per test and only a name at an integer is rewritten (chain of 48: 731 ms against 699 ms plain); tests `l01_39_reduced_product::follow` (5, with scaling at n and 8n). (b) `(1, 2)` at `(f64, f64) \| (String, f64)` was rejected because two shapes have length 2; a literal component now adopts the one carrier that component holds across the shapes of its length, nested too (spec clause in wt/lit-adopt-spec dd50abd); tests in `l01_26_type_resolution::literals` and a batch unit in l01_29. (c) "adopts no one carrier" names the carriers in printed order (`rat and f64`, `1r` or `1.0`). Found, not fixed: the plain nested-condition chain in `product::arms` is about d^2.4 (COMPLEXITY F70, item PRODUCT-nested-chain). Repair #2 (verifier's defects): (a) `x == 1` with `x: f64 \| rat` was accepted and false at `1.0` and `1r`: `Ck::follow` now decides by `carriers_in`, the rule of a parameter, so it is rejected ("adopts no one carrier", naming `1r` and `1.0`), and `x == 1` at `int \| f64` keeps int (so `f(1.0)` is false there, as the spec says). (b) `p == (1, 2)` with `p: (f64, f64) \| (String, f64)` was false at `(1.0, 2.0)`: `==` paired components only at exactly one tuple shape; `sides` now keeps each component's types across every shape of the length, as `adopted` does. (c) A tuple component at two carriers, neither its own (`(1, "a")` at `(f64, String) \| (rat, String)`), now gets the "adopts no one carrier" message from `adopted`, the same as a bare literal, instead of the generic one; the separate bare-literal check at the parameter became dead and was removed. Spec clause for `==` in wt/lit-adopt-spec b1719c5. Tests: two in `l01_26_type_resolution::literals` (15 tests now), `==` added to the shapes scaling test, two batch units in l01_29. Still as documented: `outer2(x) = { g(positional y) = y == x; g(1) }` is false at `1.0`, because `g`'s parameter is an open variable and a literal argument there keeps its own carrier; the same program with `x: f64` declared agrees. |

| ISSUE1-else-atoms | merged and pushed (7e5657d9, d4492e23, ddd6fb4a, 3a5d6f07, 2f63560d, f9fb8e6b, 0ba6b058, 52b246ed, 253e00bb, c3044ee8, rebased on main 62d5c21a, and the commit that records this; spec d5fe842, 1c37182, 22bd599, ef1ec9e) | An undeclared parameter used as the operand of `else`, or tested by `is #a`, was bounded to carriers only, so a loose atom passed the call by (rule 6 of sec:type_resolution) and the body's handling of it never ran: `r(positional line) = trim(line else "")` with `r(#notFound)` gave `#notFound`. Now the resolver records each `else` and `is` whose operand is a parameter's name (`Resolution::handles`, `resolve::Handle`), and the checker joins the atoms they handle per parameter (`check::handled`): every loose atom for `p else d`, `#a` with any payload for `p else #a -> d`, `#a` for `p is #a`. The parameter's type in the body and in its signature is its class's type joined with those atoms (`param_ty`, `handled_tys`, at `InferEnd` and `LambdaEnd`); a class that already holds every value stays a type variable, and the class's own type is unchanged, so `else` still strips what it strips. The issue's function is `(String \| #_) -> String`, `(line else 0) + 1` is `(int \| #_) -> int`, `add(a, b) = (a else b) + b` gives `a` the atoms beside its number carrier. A name bound to a parameter, `s = line` (also through a chain, `t = s; s = line`), handles atoms for it: the resolver records each such binding (`Resolution::aliases`) and `check::handled` joins the binders in a union-find before joining the atoms per class; a name bound to a computed value (`s = trim(line)`) handles nothing for `line`. The atoms are recorded per parameter in the checker, not held on the union-find classes of the type checker as decided: class-held atoms gave `(line else 0) + 1` the type `(int \| #_) -> int \| #_`. Printed signatures (T6-kinds syntax, each read back by `check::read::parse_type`): the issue's rows 1, 2 and 5 are `(_) -> "s" \| #absent`, `(_) -> "" \| #absent \| _`, `(_) -> "" \| _` (an unbounded type variable printed `_`, as PRINT-tyvars prints it); row 3 is `(String \| #_) -> String \| #absent` (not `#notFound`, since the body also has `line else ""`, as the rule gives); rows 4 and 6 are `(String \| #_) -> String`. Origin notes for a binding and a narrowing now print their type only when a message shows them; formatting it eagerly was quadratic in a chain of names (45.9 times the bytes at 8n). Tests: `l01_26_type_resolution::handled` (15, signatures by subtyping and the exact printed forms), `l01_16_elaboration::handled` (7, values of the issue's rows and the issue's program run whole), the two scaling tests (COMPLEXITY.md F74). Red before green: with the atoms not recorded, 13 of the first 19 fail; with aliases not recorded, 4 fail. Behavior change: `if line is #notFound {""} else {trim(line)}` called with a line of stdin ran before and is now rejected, since the parameter is `String \| #notFound`, as a declared parameter of that type is; the message also lists `#notFound` among the atoms `r` does not take, which is wrong and belongs to CLASS-atoms (rule 6 is all-or-nothing per argument). No existing expected printed signature changed. Repair 2 (verify 2, blocking 1): with one arrow `(String \| #_) -> String \| #_`, a body that handles the atoms and also passes them on (`if (line else "") == "" { "empty" } else { trim(line) }`, and the `bool` and `int` forms `{ d = c else false; if c {..} else {..} }`, `{ d = n else 0; if n > 3 {..} }`) gave `r(" a ")` a result a `Lines` port refused; those programs ran on main. Now such a function has two arrows, `((String) -> String) & ((String \| #_) -> String \| #_)`: the first, for an argument with no atom, gives the loose atoms that reach the result from elsewhere than the parameter (`Ck::outside`, a set per expression computed beside `unnamed`, F75); the second takes the handled atoms. There is one arrow when no atom of the parameter reaches the result, or when the parameter's class holds other values. A call selects the first arrow that takes its arguments' atoms (`Ck::first_taking`, then `apply_one` on that arrow); an argument whose type is a class of the function being inferred takes the first arrow, so `g(positional x) = r(x)` is `(String) -> String` and an atom passed to `g` passes it by, as on main; a variable of an enclosing signature, or a dynamic type, takes the second. The reader of printed types read `(A) & (B)` of two function types as `A` alone (`Ty::meet` keeps the first side's functions, a pre-existing flaw); an intersection of function types of one arity now reads as one signature of all their arrows, so the printed two-arrow signatures read back. Tests: `l01_16_elaboration::handled` (+3: the verifier's three programs run at a `Lines` port, arrow selection with a function and a lambda, the wrapper `g`), `l01_26_type_resolution::handled` (+2: the printed two-arrow signatures read back, an atom of the body's own stays on the first arrow), `l01_26_type_resolution::atom_scaling::a_chain_of_atoms_from_elsewhere_than_the_parameter_scales`; the two older scaling tests now name the arrow they read. Verify 2 blocking 2 (scaling tests panic off Linux, `tests/src/scaling.rs:138`) is pre-existing and filed as PERF-portable, not fixed here. Also: `perf-event2` is a Linux-only dependency, and `zed_tests::scaling` builds off Linux with no counter (checked by clippy with the condition switched to macOS, not by a macOS build). Open: the payload forms `is #e(int)` and `else #e(v) -> d` are not read by the resolver; they cannot be written before L2.4 and L2.5, which must add them (ISSUE1-payload); printing of `#_` (ISSUE1-print) is done here, by T6-kinds; a type variable with `else` (`(line else 0) + bump` is `(T, bump: int) -> int` and fails at run time on a string) is present on main and recorded as ISSUE1-tvar-else. Repair 3 (verify 3, blocking): a function that handles its parameter's atoms and passes it to a two-arrow function (`g(positional x) = if (x else "") == "-" { "dash" } else { r(x) }`) got one arrow `(String \| #_) -> String \| #_`, because `outside_of` took the result atoms of every arrow of the callee; `g(" a ")` at a `Lines` port was rejected, and ran on main. Now the call contributes only the result of the arrow its arguments' outside atoms select (`Ck::outside_arrow`, `Ck::taking`, shared with `first_taking`), so `g` has the first arrow `(String) -> String`. Tests: `l01_16_elaboration::handled` (+1, two forms of `g` run at a `Lines` port) and `l01_26_type_resolution::atom_scaling::a_chain_of_functions_passing_a_handled_parameter_on_scales` (signatures of a chain of three, and scaling, F75); both fail with the fix disabled. Not done here: on the second arrow the result is `#_`, not the atoms the argument holds (less precise, sound) Repair 4 (verify 4, blocking 1): the method form `g(positional x) = if (x else "") == "-" { "dash" } else { x.r() }` gave `g` one arrow, and `g(" a ")` at a `Lines` port was rejected; so were `x?.r()`, `x.r().r()`, `f = r; f(x)` and a local lambda called on `x`, all of which ran on main. `outside_of` took the arrow selection only for a call whose callee is the name of a declared function, and `outside_arrow` placed no receiver. Now `Ck::called` resolves the callee as `call` and `member_end` do (a name, or a member `x.r` / `x?.r`, bound to one binder whose type is one function, declared or a value) and gives the receiver, which `outside_arrow` puts in the first parameter, as `call_sig` does (with no atom for `?.`, which passes the receiver's atoms by), and which `outside_of` passes on as a part. Tests: `l01_16_elaboration::handled::a_method_call_passes_its_receiver_as_the_first_argument` (8 programs; red before green: the four method forms with the arrow selection failed on the branch before the fix, `method_handles_then_passes_on` first), two scaling tests (F75). Left open, non-blocking: the second arrow's result holds `#_` rather than the argument's own atoms. Repair 5 (verify 5, blocking): a method call without parentheses, `x.r`, `x.r.r` and `{ d = x else ""; x.r }`, gave `g` one arrow, and `g(" a ")` at a `Lines` port was rejected; so were `ap(r, x)` through a higher-order `ap(positional f, positional v) = f(v)` and the computed callee `(if true { r } else { r })(x)`, all of which ran on main. `outside_arrow` read the arrow selection from the callee as written, and these callees are not among those it reads. Now each call inside a function with a widened parameter records the loose atoms it gives from outside (`Ck::tried`): when an argument holds a loose atom, the call is applied once more with each such argument cut to its atoms from outside (`Ck::outside_args`, `Ck::try_outside`, an undo-log trial like those of `Ck::repairs`), and the trial's result atoms are recorded; otherwise the call records its own result atoms. A call of a value of several functions joins the records of each; a call of a type variable or a dynamic value records unknown, so all its atoms. The check of a need replayed at a call (`replaying`) records nothing, since its calls are not in the text. `outside_of` takes the record for a call or a member; `outside_arrow` is gone. A bare member `x.trim` of a prelude word takes the word's rule, as `trim(x)` does. Tests: `l01_16_elaboration::handled::a_method_call_passes_its_receiver_as_the_first_argument` (+4: `x.r`, `x.r.r`, `x.r` after a binding, `x.trim`) and `a_function_value_called_with_a_handled_parameter_gives_a_string_for_a_string` (new, 3: `ap(r, x)`, the computed callee, a wrapper `g` over `ap` that handles nothing); red before green: both tests failed at their new rows on the branch before the fix. Scaling: `many_calls_passing_a_handled_parameter_on_scale` covers the three new call forms, and `a_chain_of_method_calls_without_parentheses_scales` is new (F75). Not fixed, present on the branch since its first commit and not on main: inside a handler, a lambda passed through a higher-order function, `ap((positional y) -> trim(y), x)`, is rejected ("`f` does not take (#_)"), since a need checked at a call has no argument span and so no rule 6 pass-by; main ran it because there `x` held no atom, and main rejects the same call on `stdin[2]` directly. Recorded as ISSUE1-lambda-need. Repair 6 (verify 6, blocking 1 and 2): rule 6 passed an argument's loose atoms by only when every arrow refused all of them, so a body that tests for one atom, `if line is #notFound { "nf" } else { trim(line) }` (the parameter `String \| #notFound`), and `trim(line else "")` were rejected when called with `stdin[2]` at a `Lines` port, whose element may also be other atoms; a declared parameter `(positional line: String \| #notFound)` was rejected the same way, also on main. Now pass-by is per atom: the atoms every arrow omits pass by, the rest stay in the argument and run the body, and `Info::passes` records the set each argument passes (`Ck::refused`, `Ck::pass_at`; the elaborator tests the argument with `is` that set before the call, `elab::walk::pass_end`). Blocking 2 (ISSUE1-lambda-need): `ap((positional y) -> trim(y), x)` inside a handler, alone or after `d = x else ""`, was rejected on the branch and ran on main. Of the two choices, (a) pass the refused atoms by at the higher-order call and (b) keep `x` unwidened, this repair takes (a), which follows rule 6 per atom: a need replayed at a call records the atoms the lambda refuses (`Ck::need_passed`), and the call that gave the lambda passes them by at the argument whose parameter holds the need's type variables (`Ck::pass_needs`); an atom that no argument of the call gives is still rejected (`ap(f) = f(#foo)`: "`f` does not take (#foo)"), and a need checked at a function's own body with no such call is rejected as before. Behavior change: `ap((positional y) -> trim(y), stdin[2])` now runs and gives the atom (main rejected it). Tests: `l01_16_elaboration::handled` (+4: the is-only, pattern-else and declared bodies at a `Lines` port, an omitted atom passing by beside a tested one, four lambda forms through `ap` and `ap2`, the rejected `f(#foo)`); red before green: on the branch before the fix the first five probe programs were rejected (and the declared one and the direct `ap` on main). Scaling: `many_calls_passing_some_atoms_of_their_argument_by_scale` and `a_chain_of_functions_each_testing_an_atom_of_its_own_scales` (F76). Still open, non-blocking: the second arrow's result holds `#_` rather than the argument's own atoms. |

The planner was still of order k·d² on a nest d deep: each region asked again whether the regions under it consume each argument, recomputed their uses, and followed each capture out region by region (instructions, nested `if`s reading 4 names: 156 to 46,812 M at d = 100 to 1,600, 4.1 times per doubling). `Planner` now numbers each region occurrence once, by its parent occurrence, node and region index (`Planner::sub`), and keeps by that number whether it consumes each argument, its uses, a lambda body's parameter modes, and the lambda each captured argument resolves to, every argument passed on the way included; the contexts stack and its truncation are gone. The plans are unchanged. What remains above linear is the paths naming the plan's regions, O(d²) bytes in all, which T14-paths replaces: with them replaced by a constant, the work doubles exactly with d. The `let c = c.clone()` in the machine's `apply` is removed. Tests `l01_34_memory::chains`: a call chain borrowed down to a borrowing leaf and owned down to a consuming one (depth 4), capture chains of `if`s and lambdas, nested `if`s owning their names down to the tuple, two wrappers with one body copied or shared in both orders (Q10), and scaling at n and 8n (11.7, 7.6, 10.95, 10.4 and 7.6 times; control, the old planner run once: 71.2 times, 60.2 times the bytes, 24.4 times, the call chain linear before). The memory plan named each region by its path from the root (`r.3/0.1/2`), a `format!` string O(d) bytes long `d` regions deep, so a nest d deep held O(d²) bytes of keys in a `BTreeMap`; the machine found each region's plan by its path, built the path of every node it ran, and gave each closure its body's path; `zed-native` built and looked up the same strings. `Plan::regions` is now a `Vec<RegionPlan>` in preorder, the root first; a `RegionPlan` has `up: Option<(up, k, i)>`, and a `NodePlan` the indices of its planned regions (`regions`) and its callee as an index (`callee: Option<usize>`). The planner keeps each occurrence's index (`Occ::at`) and sets the callees once every region is planned; `Planner::region` no longer copies its `args` (the pass-by-value site moved here from T14-low). The machine finds a region from its parent's in O(1) (`Machine::sub`), numbers node occurrences by plan index, keeps a closure's lambda as its region index and node, and takes the parts of a check's message as `&dyn Display` from `format_args!`; `Tables::visit` walks the same links and `lambda_at` is a `Vec` by plan index. A path is printed only in a message (`PathOf`, `NodeAt`, `Plan::path`), and every message text is unchanged; `Plan::find` and `Index<&str>` serve the tests. Tests: `l01_34_memory::chains` nest and capture chains now `check_order` (instructions and bytes), `l01_34_memory::scaling::planning_and_running_a_deep_nest_is_linear_in_its_depth` power 1 (was power 2), and the plan tests read regions through `Plan::find`. Negative control, the path strings put back (not committed): 41.0, 41.0, 57.1 and 50.7 times the bytes against the limit of 24 |

| AND-fns | merged and pushed (240bfcc6, 42b40566, cb992e04, rebased on main 6ba12c8a, and the commit that records this; spec 5be8ce5, e7b9d75) | COMPLEXITY.md F82. `((x: int) -> String) & ((x: f64) -> String)` read back by `parse_type` as one signature of two arrows, as DESIGN.md sec:type_language point (2) says ("it is notation for that list"); before, `Denote` met the two function types as a meet, and the printed overload did not read back as itself. The rule the spec branch adds: the arrows share one parameter list (count, and the name of each parameter a call names; a positional or unnamed parameter's name is not compared) and one set of bounds; `resolve` and `parse_type` reject an intersection of function types that differ there, two operands that bound one variable by different carriers, and a bounds clause around an intersection that is an operand of another. An overload in parentheses inside another, `(A & B) & C`, is read as `A & B & C` (repair 2: before, a nested intersection was joined by arity alone, so a differing parameter name was dropped without an error, and a second bound of one name replaced the first). A mixed intersection (`int & f`) keeps the meet. A slot written as an overload takes each arrow (`l01_26_type_resolution::fits`). `fits_in` tries an equal arrow first by hash; arrows that differ still cost n·m (open: AND-fns-fit). Found, not changed: `inc(x) = x + 1` passed to an overload slot is read as `(x: int) -> int` and rejected, as on main |
| AND-fns-fit | merged and pushed (79885f50, rebased on main 8301844a, and the commit that records this) | COMPLEXITY.md F82. `check::ty::fits_in` compared each arrow of a slot's overload with every arrow of the argument when no arrow was equal. Now an `ArrowIndex` of the argument's read arrows, built once per call after the equal-arrow lookup, gives each slot arrow its candidates: per parameter position, a posting list of arrow indices for each carrier and each bare atom name the parameter type offers (`Type::atom_names`, new in zed-kernel), and the arrows whose parameter type is open (a variable, dynamic, cofinite atoms, or no such parameter). The shortest list the slot arrow's parameters need is merged with the open list and filtered by the other needed keys; `arrow_fits` runs on these in order and stops at the first that fits. The verdict is the old one, since a parameter type that offers no piece of a needed key cannot hold it; the differential test `check::ty::tests::the_index_of_arrows_gives_the_verdict_of_every_pair` checks it against the all-pairs search on random overloads (1.2 s). Scaling test `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::passing_an_overload_to_a_slot_of_other_arrows_scales` (was the probe `..._is_quadratic`): 7.9 times at n = 100 and 800; the old scan, the negative control, 57.1 times. Not fixed: `passing_an_overload_whose_arrows_share_a_parameter_piece_is_quadratic` (#[ignore], 57.6 times): every arrow offers the shared piece, so every arrow is a candidate; the tradeoff comment at `every_arrow_fits` names the Hitting Set conjecture. Finding, not fixed: `l01_26_type_resolution::fits::an_overload_that_covers_a_slots_parameter_only_together_fits` (#[ignore]) [since fixed by AND-fns-rule6; both tests are active, and the quadratic one is now `..._scales`]: `((x: int) -> String) & ((x: bool) -> String)` passed where `(x: int \| bool) -> String` is expected is rejected, though rule 6 of sec:type_resolution accepts it; the sound wider check (the slot arrow's parameter within the union of the parameters of the arrows whose result is within its result) needs an index on results, not this one. |

| AND-fns-rule6 | merged and pushed (ce7b9082, rebased on main aac0b3cd, and the commit that records this) | COMPLEXITY.md F82. Rule 6 of sec:type_resolution types an application of an overload region by region of the argument, so `((x: int) -> String) & ((x: bool) -> String)` fits the slot `(x: int \| bool) -> String`; `fits_in` refused it. Now, when no single arrow fits a slot arrow, `check::ty::covered` accepts it when, at one parameter j, its parameter lies within the union of parameter j of the arrows that hold its other parameters and return within its result. `ArrowIndex` files each arrow also under the rarest carrier or atom name its result needs, so the candidates of a slot arrow, single or together, are the arrows whose result keys lie within its result's. This is the item's description; rule 6 is wider, and the check refuses three cases rule 6 accepts, named in the tradeoff comment at `covered`: a cover split across two or more parameters (DNF tautology, coNP-complete), arrows whose parameter j holds a variable or the unknown, and overlapping arrows whose results only intersect within the slot's result, as `((x: int) -> String \| #a) & ((x: int) -> String \| #b)` for `(x: int) -> String` (the all-subsets condition). Tests: the formerly ignored `l01_26_type_resolution::fits::an_overload_that_covers_a_slots_parameter_only_together_fits` (with a run-through, a positional and a three-arrow variant), `arrows_that_cover_together_must_each_return_within_the_slots_result`, `arrows_that_cover_only_across_two_parameters_or_by_intersected_results_are_refused`; the differential tests, renamed `check::ty::tests::the_index_of_arrows_gives_the_verdict_of_every_set_of_arrows{,_on_wider_shapes}`, compare with the all-pairs search and a search over every nonempty set of at most 10 arrows (6.2 s together). The formerly ignored probe, now `l01_26_type_resolution::scaling::passing_an_overload_whose_arrows_share_a_parameter_piece_scales`: 7.9 times the bytes and the work at n = 100 and 800, load 9 to 10 (57.6 before; 24 allowed). Negative control, not committed: the result index skipping arrow 0 fails both differential tests and 15 of the 181 tests of l01_26. Not fixed: arrows that share one piece of both the slot's parameter and its result stay O(n·m) (tradeoff at `every_arrow_fits`). No spec change. Also, `unit::load`'s resolve key puts a tag byte before each unit it reads (0 a resolved import, 1 a failed one, 2 the prelude, `unit::push_read`), so a failure whose text has the eight bytes of an exports number, or the prelude next to an import of the same unit, no longer shares a key; COMPLEXITY F84. Negative controls, run once and not committed: the old corpus text fails the l02_03 test with the two errors; the untagged parts give equal keys and fail the unit test. |
| STAGE-dag | merged and pushed (30673aa4, 269ff6ee, 4abc07fd, 3b97d3d1, ef74cf85, rebased on main 6b01e145, and the commit that records this); printing split off as STAGE-print | COMPLEXITY.md F83; follows TERM-id, whose row named this follow-up. `stage::free`, the effects of a part and `stage::mix` read each part held in a `Shared` node once, keyed by `Shared::id` (`Frees::summary`), and `mix` keys what a node mixes to by its id and the bindings of the names it reads (`Maps::restrict`, a hash-consed Patricia map), so places that reach a node under the same bindings share one mixed node and the mixed term is a graph, not a tree. `Index`, `Mins` and the tree walk `kids` are gone; the build test and `Let`'s drop read the part's summary. On d nested `if`s whose two arms are one node: `mix` 16, 174, 1280 ms at d = 1000, 8000, 64000 (debug, load 32); old code 0.37, 1.39, 5.56, 23.8 s at d = 14, 16, 18, 20. Tests `l01_28_staging::dag` (scaling at d and 8d for `free`, `color`, `passed`, `mix`; output equal to the tree form's at d = 0 to 10; effects under a shared part). Repair #1: every binding not known at build has one number (`UNKNOWN`), so a body shared below two `let`s of a parameter is mixed once (was 2^d: 327692 nodes at d = 16; now 6d + 1); tests `mixing_nested_lets_of_a_parameter_over_one_body_*` at d = 1000 and 8000 and against the tree form at d = 0 to 8. Repair #2: a binding known at build is numbered by what it is (`Mixer::number`: a literal by value, a function by id and the bindings it reads), so on `let x = 0` over d nested `if`s of `let x = x + a` and `let x = x + b` over one body `mix` makes 2d + 2 nodes for a = b (was 2^d: 32781 nodes at d = 14) and d(d + 7)/2 for a != b; tests `mixing_nested_lets_of_a_built_value_over_one_body_*`. Repair #3: functions rebound on each path reading their earlier binding (`let f = (y) -> f(y) + 1` / `+ 2` over d nested `if`s over one body) were 2^d (81956 nodes at d = 14); now a function binding is numbered by its kind, a part is evaluated with stub lambdas that log the calls it makes of its function bindings, and the memo keys the part by its id and a trie of those calls and their answers (`Probe`, `Answer`, `Mixer::recall`, `Mixer::remember`), so the family gives (3d^2 + 17d - 6)/2 nodes (161 at d = 8, 14637 at d = 96; 671 ms at d = 96, debug, load 24); a call whose key or answer is not cheap to compare (an unwritten string, float zero, a family or atom) is mixed as written. Kernel: `Lookup::refuses`, a default method asked before a key is compared, since `Text`'s order writes a rope out. Tests `mixing_lets_of_composed_functions_over_one_body_scales` (d = 12 and 8d, power 2; the ignored test is removed), `a_call_the_stubs_cannot_answer_is_mixed_as_written`, `a_call_at_a_long_join_is_mixed_as_written`; three negative controls run once (COMPLEXITY F83). Repair 4 (after verify 4): the composed family's quadratic is shown to be the output's own, d(d + 1)/2 distinct `if`s (counted by structure at d = 1 to 32; argument at `Mixer::eval` and in F83); ZED_PORT_GAPS rows for the stub tables, the run log and `Lookup::refuses`, and the answer trie. Repair 5: `Report::left` printed each term left to run with `Display`, a tree, 2^d time and text on d build-stage `if`s whose arms are one node (31.5 MB, 2.2 s at d = 20); now it holds the term itself as a `Shared` (O(1) per entry when the term is held in a node) with its reason, and `Report` is no longer `Eq` (`Term` is only `PartialEq`). On that family `mix` with its report takes 1.26, 10.3, 108.5 ms at d = 1000, 8000, 64000 (test profile, load 9 to 14); control with the old `t.to_string()` 1.79 s at d = 20. Test `reporting_a_term_left_to_run_whose_arms_are_one_part_scales` (check_order power 1 at d = 1000 and 8000, the entry compared with the term by structure; its text at d = 1). Verify 6: tests `l01_28_staging::differential`, 12 cases mixing one shared part under bindings that differ in what the build knows, as built and as its tree form, with the residual program's values checked; negative controls (identity answers made one value, the memo key without bindings, call answers not recorded) each caught, the first only by the new float-zero case; the six `dag` scaling tests fail on the `stage.rs` of 61f2250d. Soundness hole fixed: a bounded variable bound to values of several carriers, as `add(x, y) = x + y` passed for `(x: int \| f64, y: int \| f64) -> int \| f64`, which failed when run. | ZIR-family-key | merged and pushed (the commit that records this) | COMPLEXITY.md Z20. The ZIR text and binary readers built a family literal by `Family::of`, which panics when a key repeats (family.rs, `expect`), while a repeated atom field already gave an error. Both now go through `zir::text::family`, which refuses the least repeated key with `the family writes the key K twice`, after the column (text) or the byte the family starts at (binary). Keys are compared as keys, so `-0.0` repeats `0.0`; a key repeated in a level below is refused when that level closes; two families under one key are refused too, not merged, since the printer never writes a key twice. Tests in `l01_31_zir::rejections` (text and binary) and a scaling test in `l01_31_zir::scaling`. |
| WRITE-prefix-scale | done (design B, by the orchestrator's decision on C29) | `Text` is now a persistent AVL tree of shared blocks (safe Rust, no `unsafe`): a join builds O(log n) nodes, nothing read is cached in a node, `Deref`/`AsRef<str>` are gone; readers walk blocks (`chunks`: compare and `==` stop at the first differing byte, hash independent of block boundaries, printing streams) or take a temporary (`flat`: casts, `lower`, `upper`, `trim`, atom names, paths, test inputs). Holding n prefixes is O(n log n) bytes (was O(n²)). COMPLEXITY C29 rewritten (the costs B adds, measured: a `foldl` of joins 17 to 37 times slower than the old O(1) join, whole reads walk each time), K16, R4, R5; ZED_PORT_GAPS entries. Found by SUB-recursive-empty-part. `types/empty.rs` `find_rep` shows a closure for a meet of morphism types whose key types are each known empty without a search (`known_empty`: the empty type, or a recursive type its group decided empty), whatever its value type, since such a closure is never called. | PRE-stack-explicit | merged and pushed (the commit that records this) | COMPLEXITY.md PRE-stack. The author's decision on PRE-stack (2026-11-04, option (b)) left the transfer recursive on a growable stack (`deeper`, `stacker::maybe_grow`); this item rewrites it with explicit stacks on the heap. `store/frames.rs` (new): a recursive analysis is a set of `async fn`s; a call that may recurse returns a `Deep`, which, awaited, hands the callee's state machine to the loop in `Stack::run`, which keeps the suspended callers in a `Vec` and runs the top one; calls run in the order of plain recursion, and a call whose answer is known takes no frame (`Stack::ready`). `store/preimage.rs`: every analysis moved from `impl Tx` to `Walk` (a `RefCell<&mut Tx>`, the translator's `Tr` when translating, and the `Stack`), in five stages: `close`; `mentions` and `reads_mod`; `constant`, `fwd`, `clip` and `summon`; `back`, `scan`, `pa`, `by_class` and `residue_test`; `lin`, `formula` and `chain`. `Tx` keeps the bridges `fwd`, `back`, `pa`, `lin`, `formula` and the lambda stack (`enter`, `leave`, `open`, `shut`, `closes`; `Tx::within` and `Tx::through` split into these). `Walk::cached` replaces `Tx::cached`, with the same memo order. `deeper`, its `// Complexity tradeoff:` comment and `stacker` are gone from `preimage.rs`, and `stacker` from `zedc`'s dependencies (the workspace keeps it for `zed-kernel` and `zed-tests`). Tests (2026-11-05, load 50 to 87): `l01_25_preimage_catalog` 202, `l01_38` 193 and 1 ignored, `l01_39_reduced_product` 134, `l01_39b` 7, `m0_10_finite_model_harness::laws_11_20` 21, `zedc` `store::` 58 (the verifier split the two randomized preimage tests, which each passed 60 s once at load 69 to 76, into four tests each by round range over the same seed and the same programs; the slowest part takes 20.4 s with all eight at once at load 62). `l01_25_preimage_catalog::deep` on a 2 MiB stack at depth 100,000 passes, and scales 8.37, 8.09 and 7.97 times in instructions from 12,500 to 100,000 (limit 24). Control, run once and not committed: an O(depth) scan of the suspended frames at each push, 49.8 times at depths 1,500 and 12,000 (fails), 8.03 without it. Cost against the `stacker` form: deep tests 1.2 to 1.8 times the time and 1.15 to 1.44 times the peak memory (chain of lets 883 to 1,274 MB); open: PRE-frame-size boxes the largest inline futures (the `back_in` frame is 1,120 bytes) and frames over 512 bytes, measured against main at depth 100,000. |

| LIT-plain | done | By the author's decision of 2026-11-04 (no implicit coercion): a plain literal adopts no carrier. Where the expected type does not hold its own carrier it is rejected with the forms to write that the type holds, as "`1` is an int where `f64` is expected; a literal takes no other carrier; write `1.0` or `1n`" (`check::menu`, `expected`; `0xFF` offers only `0xFFn`, `-3` offers `-3.0` or `-3n`). A plain literal compared with a class joins it keeping only its own carrier; a generic one follows it as before. `fewest` ranks a call's arrows by the literal errors they give, and a call so rejected gives the empty type, so the operators around it report nothing more (it gave `_`, which cascaded).
