Open: `x == 1` with `x: String` and `x == "a"` with `x: int` are accepted, as on main; DESIGN.md:1847 rejects two numbers with no common carrier and :1843 lets `==` compare values of another kind, so this is a question for the author. `if c { 1 } else { 2.5 }` prints `1 | 2.5`, as on main, where DESIGN.md:765 says `1 | f64` (LIT-if-print). A rat literal at f64 is reported by the general message, printing `0.5r` as `5e-1r`, the canonical form of `Rat::literal_text`, which DESIGN does not rule on. The span of the menu for `-3` leaves out the minus. |
| GROUP-oracle | done (wt/group-oracle, not merged; tests only) | COMPLEXITY.md K55 (K54 renumbered in batch 4: K54 is T6-2sat's). The finite-value oracle of cross-review X14 ported to Rust in `tests/steps/l01_23_subtyping/group_oracle.rs`: a system of definitions (atoms, `int`, `bool`, `empty`, `top`, tuples, morphisms with bounds, `\|`, `&`, `\`) is turned into kernel groups, one per strongly connected component, and into a pool of finite values (atoms, scalars, tuples, closures of seven body forms, applied by a deterministic evaluator that counts a repeated application as divergence), on which an alternating three-valued fixed point gives each definition a member (a proof it is inhabited), no value (a proof it is empty only when the pool holds every choice; otherwise the system is rerun at 16 times the cap, the E rule), or open. `bes_families::random(seed, rate, n)` is public to the module. Tests (14, 3.8 s together): four generators in two pieces each (the X14 family, partial morphisms with empty keys and `\ #stop`, layered groups, meets and differences over products of unions), 1,100 systems and 2,905 admitted members: no `Empty` and no `is_empty` against a shown member, no `Inhabited` against an exhaustive pool with no value; 0 reruns and 0 unsettled; 85 refusals of members the pool shows (79 in the layered groups); `seeded_random_systems_agree_with_the_reference_{0,1}`, 80 seeds over rates 0 to 18 and n 2 to 21: `decide` against `reference::answers` (24,930 answers, 30 refused), and `Type::verdict` and `is_empty` of 1,722 admitted groups (14,088 members) against it; the fixed case `W & ((empty) -> int)` (apart, an `Inhabited` and an `Empty` member; under one name the group is rejected, and the pool finds no fixed point); a morphism on a cycle (five cases); a member split across box cells (four cases); the scaling test (K54). Controls, not committed: see the T6 row. Found: the value builder refuses inhabited members with long witnesses, `R_i = (R_j, R0) \| (R_{i+1}, #stop)`, `R_{n-1} = (R_j, R0) \| #stop`, at some seeds from n = 8 (Open points); fixed by VERDICT-group-share. |
| VERDICT-group-share | done (wt/verdict-group, not merged) | COMPLEXITY.md K56. From the GROUP-oracle control. `Type::verdict` (`types/empty.rs`) decides the first member asked of a group its decider decides together with every member of the group (`group_verdicts`): one search in definition order, which keeps each value it builds, so the verdicts of all n members cost one search over the group, and each verdict is the same whichever member is asked first (`Type::group_members`, `rec::Key::members`). `Searching::rep` keeps a failure with the types whose missing values it read and seeks it again when one of them gets a value (`Searching::retry`), which fixes the refusals of long witnesses that GROUP-oracle found (the chain group). Tests: `verdicts_of_random_groups_scale` gains the verdicts of every member of one chain group and of one X14 group of n, n 128 and 1024 (17.9 and 9.8 times the instructions, limit 24); new `every_member_of_a_long_chain_is_inhabited` (512 members, all `Inhabited`, the same verdicts asked forward and backward and for five members asked alone). Control, not committed, on the base commit's `types/empty.rs`: 37.7 and 75.4 times, 1011 of 1024 chain members refused; both tests fail. Kept: a group its decider does not decide still costs a search per member, O(n²) (author question A33). Affected tests pass: zed-kernel lib 97, l01_22 41, l01_23 155, l01_24 66, l01_26 244, l01_27 48, l01_29 152. |
| VERDICT-group-roots | done (wt/verdict-group-roots, not merged) | COMPLEXITY.md K61. Author question A33 withdrawn (the standing rules decide it). `group_verdicts` (`types/empty.rs`) now decides every member of a group that the decider leaves open by one search with all of them as roots (`Search::solve_all`; `Searching::solve` takes a list of roots, seeds, walks and shares the phases), so the verdicts of all n members cost one search of the group, not n. A fork in such a search refuses only the types that read the type being decided (`Search::causes`, over the readers, O(types + reads)); one met with no type being decided ends the search as before. `Type::verdict` sends every member of a recursive group to `group_verdicts`. Verdicts of the 16,993 sweep members unchanged. Tests in `l01_23_subtyping::group_oracle`: `verdicts_do_not_depend_on_the_order_members_are_asked_in` (groups of three in all six query orders and all six definition orders, larger groups in every rotation, decided and undecided, each against the verdict asked alone after `forget_emptiness`: 554 members, 945 orders), `every_member_of_an_undecided_long_chain_is_inhabited` (512), `verdicts_of_undecided_groups_scale` (chain 9.4 times, X14 group 8.7 times; the base commit fails at 67.4 times the bytes). Kept: definitions outside a group that read an open member still each get a search of their own (follow-up VERDICT-group-readers). |
| VERDICT-group-readers | done (wt/verdict-group-readers, not merged) | COMPLEXITY.md K62. Follow-up of K61. `Type::verdict` (`types/empty.rs`) first decides, by `decide_groups_read`, every recursive group the type reads, each after the groups it reads, from an explicit stack on the heap (groups found by a walk that stops at other groups' members and notes each group once by `Type::group`). A strict search reads a member of another group by its kept verdict, as it reads `judged` (`Searching::group_verdict`), and by the values the group's searches built (`Searching::shared`, `Rep::Of`, kept in the thread-local `STORES` until `forget_emptiness`), so a definition outside a group no longer decides the group again. Scaling (`verdicts_of_undecided_groups_scale`, new shape: every definition of the undecided X14 system of n): 8.3 times the bytes, 9.3 times the instructions at 8n; the base commit 188fc1c3 fails at 65.8 times. Verdicts over 13,495 sweep definitions in three orders identical to the base, except `layer` seed 316 R3 (`\ #none`), an unsound `Empty` now `Inhabited` as the oracle shows. New tests in `l01_23_subtyping::group_oracle`: `readers_of_undecided_groups_agree_with_the_values` (4 generators, seeds 0 to 99, against the oracle and in both orders against each alone) and `a_chain_of_groups_is_decided_on_a_small_stack` (1000 groups on a 1 MiB stack; the base overflows). `Search::find_cycle` now keeps a walked cycle for every type on it, with the stop moved to that type's place, so the p members of a cycle group are decided in O(p), was O(p²) (57.3 times the bytes at 8n on the base); tests `scaling::the_members_of_two_cycles_are_decided_in_the_size_of_their_types` and `scaling::a_cycle_kept_for_each_type_on_it_gives_each_its_own_lengths`. Follow-ups: `is_empty` of layer 316 R3 still unsound (non-strict path; fixed by EMPTY-nonstrict-unsound); the meet's constant factor and the super-linear `kernel()` test helper fixed by VERDICT-meet-cost. |
| EMPTY-nonstrict-unsound | done (wt/empty-nonstrict, not merged; based on wt/verdict-group-readers) | COMPLEXITY.md K64. `is_empty` (`types/empty.rs`, the non-strict search) answered `Empty` for the inhabited R3 of layer seed 316 with `\ #none` bodies. Reduced to four definitions (`a_type_decided_again_is_not_read_as_empty_before_it_is`). Cause: `Searching::drain` took the whole `again` set into its own list, so in a later phase a type reset to `true` and still waiting in that list was read by `no_value`, and `settle` took it as dead before it was decided again. Fix: the round under way is kept in the search (`Search::round`) and popped one type at a time, so a nested drain decides the waiting types first; a type decided again by `drain` is in `deciding`. Tests (group_oracle.rs): the minimal case, `the_layered_system_316_is_not_called_empty`, four sweeps `*_never_called_empty_with_a_value` (claude, mixed, layer, cells; seeds 0 to 599, plain and `\ #none` bodies; 3,670, 1,676, 3,350 and 1,146 members with a value checked, 4 to 9 s each), and `a_chain_decided_again_scales`. The earlier sweeps' `missed` counts are unchanged. Groups l01_22, l01_23, l01_24, l01_26 to l01_29, m0_09, m0_10 and zed_kernel pass |
| VERDICT-meet-cost | merged and pushed in batch 18 (a68a99d4) | COMPLEXITY.md K65. Follow-up of K62. `Type::verdict` (`types/empty.rs`) of a type outside a recursive group now runs first only the deciders of the groups it reads (`decide_groups_read(false)`, skipping groups already judged, `Type::judged_known`), and a group's verdicts are decided when a strict search first reads one of its members; a member keeps the full pre-pass (a chain of groups needs it for stack depth). The meet of two cycles at n 1024 now costs what its `is_empty` does, 13.1M instructions and 0.90M bytes (was 87.5M and 10.5M on main, 6.7 and 11.7 times). The test helper `kernel()` (`group_oracle.rs`) is Tarjan's algorithm on an explicit stack, linear (9.3 ms for a chain of 1000 groups, was 1.57 s). New tests: `scaling::the_verdict_of_a_meet_of_two_cycles_costs_what_its_emptiness_does` (main fails at 11.7 times the bytes), `group_oracle::the_groups_of_a_system_are_built_in_its_size` (old helper fails at 27.0 times the bytes); `a_chain_of_groups_is_decided_on_a_small_stack` also asks a tuple outside the groups. |
| REP-path-memo | done (wt/empty-search, not merged) | COMPLEXITY.md K59. From GROUP-oracle: `Type::verdict` refused members the oracle shows inhabited, 84 in the committed sweeps (layered 79, X14 4, cells 1). Causes found in `types/empty.rs`, confirmed by negative controls: (1) `find_rep` built a closure for a meet of one morphism type as the meet of that type with the top morphism type, which unfolds a recursive value type into a type that is neither the one being built nor one its group decided, so the closure was refused; a meet of one morphism type is now that type as written. (2) `returnable` asked the builder for a value of the closure's value type even when the group's decider had decided that type inhabited; a closure is never called here, so such a value type now counts as returnable without a value built (this trusts the decider's `Inhabited`). (3) A failure that read a type which got its value while the failing build was still running was kept with that type's retry already past; `rep` now seeks it again at once. Refusals after: 80 (layered 79, cells 1). Tests: the sweeps of `group_oracle.rs` assert the count each refuses; new `a_closure_returns_a_member_built_or_decided_inhabited` (X14 seeds 210 and 118, every member `Inhabited`). Controls, not committed: without (2), 2 tests fail; with a meet of one type as before (1), 3 tests fail; (3) changed no count in l01_23 or in 4,800 more random systems, so it is untested (inferred reachable only through a rollback followed by a retry). Remaining refusals: see Open points (GROUP-oracle). |
| EMPTY-key-search | done (wt/empty-search, not merged) | COMPLEXITY.md K60 (K52 updated). `Type::verdict` refused `(bool -> empty) -> empty` (`NoWitness`): `find_rep` built a closure only when its key type was known empty without a search. Now, when a closure's value type gives it no value or its keys are not permitted as written, `find_rep` decides each key type in the same search (`Searching::no_keys`, `no_key`, through `no_value`), with no new search per nesting level. Found while testing it: `Search::settle` marked a change even when the type it settled was already dead, so a key type proved empty in each phase started a new phase each time and one layered group (seed 1335) never ended; `settle` now returns at once for a dead type, so each phase takes at least one more type as dead. Committed layered sweeps: refusals 28 and 51 → 4 and 3. Sweep of seeds 300 to 1,500, not committed: layered 507 → 34, mixed 9 → 4, X14 (claude) 7 → 7, cells 9 → 9. | ATOM-payload-fn | merged and pushed (the commit that records this) | COMPLEXITY.md F95. DESIGN allows a function in an atom payload (`#atom(t)`), and `==` refuses functions whose domain cannot be enumerated (Soundness); `Ty::held_function` and `unchecked_function` never looked inside payloads, so `x = #a(h); same = x == x` was accepted. Now they do (`Ty::function_in`), `Ck::data` gives the classes in payloads their need, and a `==`, `!=` or `String` refusal derives from the operand, so it shows where the function came from (a new note, "this atom `#a` with its payload"). Tests: `l01_17_value_representation::equality` (4 new), `l01_29_diagnostics::derivations` (1), `l01_26_type_resolution::held_functions` (2, one scaling). Open: (1) `held_wide` exists only on wt/atom-payload-sub and needs the same payload walk when both merge; (2) `Row::open` ignores variables in payloads, so `f(positional x) { return #a(x), 1; }` then `p = f(x); p == p` in another function, called with a lambda, is still accepted (ledger ATOM-payload-open); (3) atoms nested directly in atoms are O(n^2) in the checker, before this item too; fixed by ATOM-nested-sq (F106) |
| OMEGA-cycle-growth | merged and pushed (the commit that records this) | Repair found by NAME-orbit-test. The 4-cycle `x0 >= x1 >= x2 >= x3 >= x0` in `[-1, 1]^4` with `1 <= x0 + x1 + x2 + x3 <= 3` was refused `Growth`: `Omega::run` entered its loop with 14 rows and took no step (every elimination had fill 10, four live variables, no inexact variable), and the box stage found neither emptiness nor a point. Now `Omega::cycles` runs once, before the first step that is not an equality: the rows `x - y + k >= 0` with `k <= 0` (and one-variable rows through a node for zero) are edges, and the rows of each strongly connected component (Tarjan, `utvpi::components`, now `pub(super)`) become equalities, solved as those of the input; an edge of weight above zero in a component shows the system empty. O(m log m + n). The orbit test now asserts `Ok(false)`; new tests `l01_38_presburger::omega::a_cycle_of_rows_makes_its_variables_equal` and `l01_38_presburger::scaling::a_cycle_of_rows_scales` (512 and 4,096 variables: 9.4 times the instructions, 8.0 times the bytes, load 57 to 59). Controls, not committed: the search disabled fails all three tests with `Growth`; the components found again for each equality fails the scaling test (38.2 times the bytes). Not found, recorded as a complexity tradeoff (COMPLEXITY.md OMEGA-cycle-growth): zero-sum cycles with weights of both signs, and cycles that form only after a step. |
| LOWER-legalize | merged and pushed (the commit that records this; spec cfacf66, rebased from 0157f5a) | COMPLEXITY.md Z25 (Z22, Z23 and Z24 taken by ZIR-name-binary, NATIVE-lib-pick and ZIR-text-binders). Merge (2026-11-05): rebased over PRIM-op-names, which removed `zir::text::spell`, so the code generator's refusal of an applied `>` names it by `Prim::printed`, and the kernel now writes an operator as the program does, so the expected refusal reads `` `>` applied to `int`, `String` `` (was `Gt`). ZIR-name-binary landed before the push: its binary reader is what a built executable decodes at start, so l01_36a and `exe::tests::the_data_reads_back` were rerun on it. Verified on the rebased tree: l01_36 legalize 11 (scaling included) and its 17 other modules, l01_36a 11, l01_17 72, deep_heap 22, `zedc --lib zir::` 4, `zed-native --lib` 9, at load 64 to 93; control rerun: an empty swapped set in the hooks gives `` `<` applied to `String`, `int` `` and fails. Repair 1 (2026-11-05): a refusal names the operator and operands as written. `legalize` returns a `Legalized` graph, the region and a bit set of the nodes it swapped by their number in preorder; `zed_native::run`, `run_at` and `object` take only a `Legalized`, so an unlegalized graph cannot reach the code generator. Native's slow path (`hooks::kernel`, `hooks::term`) raises a swapped node, or one in its regions, by `raise_node` as `>` or `>=` on its inputs in written order, so `1 > "Oslo"` gives `Gt applied to int, String` on the kernel, the machine and natively, and an order refused its memory names `>` or `>=`. Tests added to `l01_36_cranelift::legalize` (11 now): `a_refused_comparison_names_the_operator_and_operands_as_written` (plain, inside a `fam`, inside a closure) and `an_order_refused_its_memory_names_the_operator_as_written` (a 2^30-bit constant, built as ZIR by hand because lowering a constant that wide takes minutes: its content address writes the number in decimal). After the rebase onto step L1.36a, `zedc build` legalizes too: `executable_object` takes the lowered region and `fuse`, writes the region as lowered into the executable's data, and legalizes and plans it itself; the executable's start legalizes the decoded graph before planning, so its tables and swapped set equal the build's. Test `l01_36a_executables::agreement::a_built_comparison_by_greater_than_agrees` (strings compared by `>` and `>=` from the command line, built twice, equal to `zedc run`), and `exe::tests::the_data_reads_back` now reads back a graph holding `>` and checks its swapped set. Negative controls, run once: an empty swapped set in the hooks failed both new legalize tests (`Lt applied to String, int`; `<` must compare); numbering only the top node failed the `fam` case; an empty expansion set in `executable_object` failed the build test with "native: `prim >` has no lowering here". Groups run: l01_36 all modules (legalize 11, scaling 23, hooks 11 and the rest), l01_36a 11, l01_17 72, l01_34 29, l01_37 7, `deep_heap` 22, `zedc --lib zir::` 4, `zed-native --lib` 9, all pass. `zedc::zir::legalize`: one pass after lowering and before `memory::plan` applies the expansions a back end declares as a constant `Expansions` set: `Derived` puts a derived node's certificate in its place, `Gt` and `Ge` rewrite an applied `a > b` and `a >= b` as `b < a` and `b <= a` (a primitive with no inputs, a function value, is kept). `memory::EXPANSIONS` = {Derived}; `zed_native::EXPANSIONS` = {Derived, Gt, Ge}, and `zed_native::legalize` runs it; `through` and the native test helpers legalize before planning. The code generator no longer has `>` and `>=` in its compare tables and refuses an applied one by name ("native: `prim >` has no lowering here; legalization expands it (gt)"). The bytes per node of the four representations, measured on the step units by the counting allocator (`zed_alloc_count::live`: net and peak bytes of one thread while a closure runs; the bytes of a structure are those its drop frees) and recorded in `zed/compiler/MLIR.md`, section Layout: syntax tree 255 bytes per expression or statement, kernel terms 158 bytes per distinct term, ZIR 935 bytes per node (inline `Node` 112), native values a 16-byte word plus a 144-byte cell when boxed (195 cells in 109 runs). | FLOAT-tie-read | done (test and STATUS only) | Why a float tie prints the larger magnitude (DESIGN.md sec:reduction item 1, "Of two shortest digit strings equally near the value, printing takes the one of larger magnitude"), read in the std source of the toolchain in use, rustc 1.99.1 (Fedora; the files moved to `library/core/src/num/imp/flt2dec/strategy/`, logic unchanged from 1.93.0). `zed_kernel::num::show_f64` takes its digits from `{:e}`, which calls `grisu::format_shortest`. Its `format_shortest_opt` ends in `round_and_weed`, which returns `None` (grisu.rs:442; 440 in 1.93.0) when the digits nearest `v` plus its error bound are not also nearest `v` minus it, as at an exact tie, and the fallback `dragon::format_shortest` decides: `if up && (!down \|\| *mant.mul_pow2(1) >= scale)` (dragon.rs:247; 245 in 1.93.0) rounds up when twice the remainder is at least the scale, so exactly one half goes up, in magnitude, since the sign is apart. Probe (RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP, `num_internals`, not committed): on 104,000 constructed ties `format_shortest_opt` returned `None` every time and Dragon gave the larger form every time; on 100,000 random floats Grisu succeeded on 99,596. Where ties are: `v = M * 2^-p` is halfway between `D * 10^-j` and `(D+1) * 10^-j` only when `M = K * 2^t`, `K` odd, `t = p - j - 1`, both read back when `10^j >= 2^p`, and they are the shortest only if `10^(j-1) < 2^p`; `0 <= t <= 51` then confines ties to `2^-23 <= \|v\| < 2^51`, in 52 of the 74 binades there; at a binade's lower end, where the float below is nearer, there is one more, `2^-25` (printed `...313e-8`, the larger; found at merge by a scan of every binade and every `t`, and checked on its own by the test). In units of `10^-j / 2`, `v` is `K * 5^j`, an odd multiple of 5, always 5 units from the nearest multiple of `10^-(j-1)`, so a binade has ties exactly when `2^p <= 10^j < 5 * 2^p`, and then every float `K * 2^t * 2^-p` in it is one; the test checks this rule on every sample. Test `l01_18_scalar_interpreter::floats::every_sampled_tie_prints_the_larger_magnitude` replaces the 8,000,000-sample program that was never in the repository: 1,000 draws from a fixed seed in each binade the condition allows, each checked in exact `u128` arithmetic (halfway, both neighbors read back, whether a nearest multiple of `10^-(j-1)` does, against the binade rule); 52,000 ties in 52 binades, each with both texts read back through `f64(s)` bit for bit and `String` printing `D+1` (and `-` before it for `-v`); 0.45 s, linear in the samples. Negative control, run once and reverted: `show_f64` rounding ties down fails this test (`1180954526937006.75` printed `...006.7`) and the 1666666666666669.25 test; the other 48 of l01_18 pass. Changed at merge: the halfway check read `K` from the sampler, odd by construction, so it could not fail; it now reads `M` from `v`'s bits, and a sampler built one below each tie fails it (it failed only the later read-back check before); the tie at `2^-25` was added; rounding ties down fails both the sampled ties and the `2^-25` check. Flaw named, not fixed: there is no rust-toolchain file, so the rule rests on std internals; this test is what would catch a change. Groups run: l01_18 50 pass 1 ignored (load 64 to 46); `cargo fmt --all` and `cargo clippy -p zed-tests --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean (no other crate changed). |

| INCR-script | merged and pushed (the commit that records this; tests only) | COMPLEXITY.md row INCR-script. An edit-script format for the query cache of L1.30, in `l01_30_queries/reuse.rs`: a script is text whose `%% step`, `%% write`, `%% replace` … `%% with` and `%% delete` lines edit a set of files, run with one database kept across the steps; after each step every unit's checks (verdicts and diagnostics, `test_in`) and the package's report, unit order and each unit's resolution (`unit::load`, every `.zed` file a root) equal a fresh database's. Check-only: no built program is compared. `every_answer_equals_a_fresh_databases` is now a script of the same six texts. Corner cases: a type error added then removed (the fixed text computes nothing and checks as before), a renamed definition (the importer is resolved again and reports the missing name, then follows), a changed import edge (an import added, moved to another unit with the first unit deleted, a cycle made and removed, an import of a missing unit); two malformed scripts stop with their own messages. A unit with imports is checked alone by `test_in`, which reports its imports as needing a manifest, so its own checks say little until a package test runner exists; the package's report and resolutions are what those steps compare. Scaling 8 and 64 steps: 7.2 times the instructions and the bytes (limit 24, load 49). Negative controls, run once and not committed: rows keyed by the check alone, without the definitions it reaches, fail 3 tests (2 of them scripts); a resolution keyed by its text alone, without its imports' exports, fails `a_renamed_definition` and `a_changed_import_edge` and no older `l01_30` test; a runner doing i fresh checks at step i fails the scaling test at 41.9 times the bytes. Group run: l01_30 15 pass |

Rung 2 translates `a div n` and `a mod n` for a divisor `n` that is not a constant: quotient `q` and remainder `r` auxiliary, `a == n * q + r` with `n * q` a product atom (`Lin::product` now takes auxiliary variables as factors, through `presburger::Factor`), one disjunct per sign of `n` with `0 <= r <= \|n\| - 1`, and `q`, `r` free at `n == 0`. Merge verify: no compiler path calls `entails` (`run.rs` `linear_questions` decides each relation alone; `product.rs` translates without products and keeps only relations with no auxiliary variable), so no production path was unsound; translated in two calls through the public API, `x mod 2 == 1` to `x mod 4 <= 1` and `x mod n == 1` to `x mod m <= 1` were both proved, so `entails` now panics when the numbers of the two relations' definitions overlap (O(1): the numbers ascend, so the ends are compared), tests `relations_translated_in_two_calls_are_refused_by_entails` and `remainders_by_two_distinct_divisors_are_not_confused`; negative control, not committed: one copy of the definitions per call gives 29.5 times the instructions from 256 to 2048 (limit 24). | ORACLE-chain | ready on `wt/oracle-chain` (tests, test helpers and one zedc accessor; no spec change) | COMPLEXITY.md F107. `zed_tests::oracle::bindings` runs a unit's first-order bindings as one tuple closed over their cones (`oracle::outcomes`), so each definition is evaluated once: a chain unit (`x_i` reads `x_(i-1)`), measured at 58.5x the bytes at 500/4000 in the interpreter and 59.3x natively at 25/200 before, is now 8.6x instructions, 7.6x bytes and 8.1x, 7.7x. | ORACLE-stop-share | ready on `wt/oracle-stop-share` (tests and test helpers only; no spec change) | COMPLEXITY.md F107. `oracle::Runs` cost O((n + s·F) log n) for `s` stops reaching `F` definitions with a function component, as each stop's run binds those definitions again: `n` stops each reaching `n` functions measured 67.7x the bytes at 100/800 on origin/main e023ad8b. The decided fix, the unit's functions built once in an environment each run starts from, is not possible in the test crate: `run::Through` takes a closed term, the `Evaluator` holds no bindings from one run to the next, and the native code compiles each term into a JIT module freed after its run (a stopped native run also leaves cells live, NATIVE-stop-cells). So the fallback: the walk ends at a run, after a stop, that stops and binds again a definition with no literal that a run before that stop bound (`End::Again`, flag `Runs::again`, set by `run`); the bindings of the prefix before it get their values, each later definition that reads a known stop takes its error, and the rest are `Known::Unrun`, each binding among them given `Outcome::NotCompared`, with the reason. New `Report::not_compared` lists them ("binding `b1`: not compared, as the run of the definitions from `b1` on, after the stop at `b0`, binds again definitions with a function component, and stops"); an engine that gives such a binding an outcome is a mismatch. O(n log n) (Complexity tradeoff comment at `Runs`, "(author question A32)" removed). Nothing changes before that end: `alone.rs` checks the old units against the per-binding runs as before, and `l01_37_oracle::oracle` now asserts that no fence, step unit or corpus program leaves a binding uncompared. Tests (`l01_37_oracle` `alone.rs`): `stops_that_reach_functions_scale`, 100/800, 10.1x the bytes and under 10.5x instructions (red 67.7x); `a_second_stop_through_functions_is_not_compared`, the expected mismatches and not-compared lines of the unit at 3 plus a binding after the end that reads the first stop (interpreter engines only: natively the stopped runs leave the closures live, NATIVE-stop-cells). Negative control, not committed: the bisection at every stop in place of the end gives 67.7x the bytes and fails the three tests. Groups (load5 21 to 57): l01_21 54, l01_36a 21, l01_37_oracle 23, l01_37a 17; clippy -D warnings and fmt clean |

The counting machine (`zir/memory.rs`) copied the path above each binder of a match, read each binder's cell from the top of its path, and in a fused release filtered and read the kept paths again at every level: O(d²) for a binder at each of d levels, the flaw HEAP-binder-paths fixed in `zed_rt`. | T6-2sat | verified, ready for the batch merge on branch wt/t6-2sat (not merged) | COMPLEXITY.md K54. A tuple part that is a 2-CNF over two-valued components is decided by Tarjan's strongly connected components of its implication graph, in O(V + E) with an explicit stack, and recognized by structure: each open tuple type open on one component, each component cut into one piece and its complement, each clause of at most two literals (`types/empty.rs` `Searching::two_sat`, `read_two_sat`, `TwoSat::solve`; `Part::id` and `Bdd::id` in `bdd.rs`). `tuples_on` uses it before the per-length search, and `find_rep` takes the witness from it, the box cut to the pieces the solution chooses. The x_tail.rs cnf probe on main first: no refusal on r2, chain, chainsat or xorcycle (Search::refine, K35), but xorcycle cost about n^3.5 (22.8 s at 129 variables); after: 7.8 ms. Tests `l01_23_subtyping::two_sat` (9) and two kernel unit tests in `empty.rs`. Scaling (merge verification, orchestrator's decision): n is the type's size, of order the variables squared, so the chain, the open chain and the parity cycle run at 45 and 128 variables (types 8 times apart) within the standard 24; the search before this item is n^1.5 in the type and passes that too (22 times), so a fourth test measures the parity cycle by its variables, 11 and 88, within 24 (8.9 times; the control fails it at 446 times the bytes). | DIAG-suppress | merged and pushed in batch 4 (815b7763, 796d8a6e, f5e0de38, b211c206; spec a5de1dc) | COMPLEXITY.md row DIAG-suppress. Each `Diagnostic` carries an address (`crates/zedc/src/suppress.rs`): BLAKE3-256 of the severity, the rejection row, the enclosing top-level declaration's name, the node's text with white space dropped, the message and the occurrence count among diagnostics with all of these equal; line and column are left out, so a line added above keeps it. `zedc run`, `replay`, `build` and `test` print `  address: <64 hex>` under each warning, and under each rejection where the unit is loaded by `load_file` (run, replay, build). `--suppress <address>` (repeatable) on those four commands leaves that warning unprinted and the program and its ZIR unchanged; naming a rejection is an error (`names a rejection, which cannot be suppressed`) and the run fails with status 1; an address that names nothing is reported and the run goes on; a word that is not 64 lowercase hex digits is a usage error, status 2. Tests: `l01_29_diagnostics::suppress` (9). Not done: `zedc test` prints rejection outcomes without addresses; `Package::report` and the refusals of `program()` print none; the address names only the top-level declaration, not a full keypath. Negative controls, not committed: `hides` as a linear scan fails `matching_suppressions_scales` at 62.9x; a suppression that changes the program fails the ZIR comparison. |
| DIAG-suppress-rest | merged and pushed in batch 6 (1ce2c0e5, rebased on main d76e807c, and the commit that records this; spec 730b2d6) | COMPLEXITY.md row DIAG-suppress-rest. A printed address now names the file: `suppress::InFile::of(path)` hashes the path from the current directory to the file, both resolved by `fs::canonicalize` (links, `.` and `..`), as one `..` per remaining part of the current directory and then each part of the file's path, each ended by a zero byte; `InFile::address(d)` is BLAKE3 of that hash and `Diagnostic::address`, which stays the part the text decides, so the front end's memo by text is untouched. `zedc run`, `replay`, `build` (`load_file`) and `zedc test` print and match the joined address; a file or current directory that cannot be resolved is status 2. Two files of one text have two addresses, any paths to one file one, hard links two; a tree copied elsewhere and run from the same place prints the same addresses, and running from another directory prints others (queued as DIAG-addr-root: the deterministic-checking rule asks for addresses that do not depend on where `zedc` runs). Tests: `l01_29_diagnostics::suppress` (12: two files and two directories with one text, five paths to one file including a symbolic link, a name with spaces, a comma, `ü`, `&`, `$` and quotes; `zedc test` batch against each file alone), `zed-native` test `suppress_binary` (2, the executable run with `current_dir`). Not done, as before: `zedc test` rejection outcomes, `Package::report` and `program()` refusals print no address; the address names the top-level declaration, not a full keypath. |

Tests: `l01_29_diagnostics::rejection_addresses` (8). | DIAG-addr-root | merged and pushed in batch 35 (5fc72dd5, da6e94c6; `crates/zedc/Cargo.toml`, `src/lib.rs`, `place.rs`, `run.rs`, `suppress.rs`, `testing.rs`, `unit.rs`); spec 08a19a2 (DESIGN sec:diagnostics, no grammar change) | COMPLEXITY.md row DIAG-addr-root. The file part of a printed address no longer depends on the directory `zedc` runs in (the deterministic-checking rule): `place::Place::of` (its own module, which `unit` and `suppress` both import, so they do not import each other) resolves the file and walks up to the nearest `zed.manifest`; a unit is named by its path from the package root, its unit name, so a copy of a package elsewhere prints the same addresses; a file with no manifest above it by its absolute resolved path, the only root that names it whatever the working directory is and keeps two files of one text apart. The two kinds are hashed under two tags. The walk opens each `..` with `rustix::fs::openat` (`O_PATH`, Linux only until PORTABLE-rt) so the cost is linear in the depth of the file. A non-UTF-8 path under a package root now ends `zedc run` with status 2 (cannot resolve the file) instead of 1. The tests that ran zedc from one directory are replaced by `suppress_binary::the_address_does_not_depend_on_the_directory_zedc_runs_in` (four directories, package and loose file) and `two_files_of_one_text_have_two_addresses` |
| ISSUE1-tvar-else | committed on wt/issue1-tvar-else, not merged | COMPLEXITY.md row ISSUE1-tvar-else. From ISSUE1-else-atoms impl#1. `r(positional line, bump = 1) = (line else 0) + bump` inferred `(_, bump: int) -> int`, so `r(" a ")` checked and failed at run time with "type error: Add applied to". The cause was not the `else`: `Ck::apply_many` merged the paths `(int, int)` and (class, int) of the sum into one state, since both kept the int arrow, and dropped the second, which carried the class's restriction. States are now also keyed by `ClassAt`, the carriers each alive arrow allows where the class stands. The signature is `(int \| #_, bump: int) -> int` and `r(" a ")` is refused at the call. Tests: `handled::a_count_or_zero_plus_a_named_step_takes_the_step_s_carrier`, `handled::a_count_or_zero_plus_another_parameter_shares_its_carrier`, scaling `sums_of_a_line_or_a_count_check_in_linear_time`. Verification with the old `apply_many`: the first handled test and the scaling test's signature check fail (`(_, bump: int) -> int`); the second handled test passes on the old code too and guards the case with both parameters undeclared. The verifier reran the negative control (a copy of `Ck::seen` per `apply_many` call): 55.1 times the bytes, limit 24, load 27. `apply_many` is reached only by operators and prelude words of several arrows (a call of a user function of 6 arguments, each `(line else 0)`, does not reach it), so its states per call stay bounded (at most 3 measured) |

| CATALOG-followups | merged and pushed in batch 28 (e6b30c2e; `crates/zedc-int/src/int.rs`, `crates/zed-tools/src/catalog.rs`, `zed/compiler/CATALOG.md`, `zed/prelude/iteration.zed`); no spec change | COMPLEXITY.md F104. Four follow-ups of PORT-catalog-audit. (1) `alloc::string::<String as Index<I>>::index` is `fromUtf8(utf8(s).slice(a..<b))` (was `.drop(a).take(b - a)`; `.slice(r)` is DESIGN.md:625 and :4410, an L5 word); the other `drop`/`take` rows take no range. (2) Zed `div` and `mod` are Euclidean, and the floor routines are reached with a negative divisor (`Num::div_floor` from `times_pre` for `x * -3`, `revision.rs:368` `quotient`), so the `div_floor` and `mod_floor` rows of `BigInt` and `i128` are `if b < 0 { (-a) div (-b) } else { a div b }` and `if b < 0 { -((-a) mod (-b)) } else { a mod b }` (were `a div b`, `a mod b`), and `BigInt::div_ceil` is `if b < 0 { -(a div (-b)) } else { -((-a) div b) }` (was `missing`). `Int::div_floor` and `Int::mod_floor` (zedc-int int.rs) were Euclidean on the 64-bit and 128-bit paths and floor on the big path, so they disagreed for a negative divisor (every caller passed a positive one); they are now floor on every path for every sign, and `MIN / -1`, whose quotient does not fit, goes to the next width (it panicked before). Unit test `int::tests::floor_division_agrees_on_every_path_for_every_sign` compares all three paths with `BigInt` at and across the ends of `i64` and `i128`, both signs of both operands; the old bodies fail it at 1 by -2 (negative control, not committed). (3) `zed/prelude/iteration.zed`: `dependencyGraph` is `for u -> _ in reads { reads.where(rs -> rs.any(w -> w == u))::domain }` (was `(for v -> rs in reads { rs.contains(u) }).keys`, which also kept every `v`, since `.keys` of a map to `bool` is its whole domain), and `solve` orders `sys.reads::domain` (was `(for v -> _ in sys.reads { true }).keys`); the `.fold`, `.distinct` and `.reverse` uses stay with PRELUDE-words, and the new `dependencyGraph` costs O(V · E), not the O((V + E) log V) of S.1e: the inversion of the edges is `group` over the edges flattened to one level, and DESIGN gives unnest (defined-forms table, DESIGN.md:4404) no word or syntax, so a `// Complexity tradeoff:` comment names this and row PRELUDE-depgraph is queued (needs CATALOG-followups and PRELUDE-words, feeds S.1e). (4) `check_words` binds a lambda's parameters (a name before its arrow, the name of a `Param` or `PositionalParam`, a tuple `Target`) only inside it, by one walk in text order with a count per name (was one set per text). Tests `s_01_port_catalog` 12 (new: `check_zed_scopes_lambda_parameters_to_their_lambda`, 6 accepted and 4 refused texts: two lambdas reusing `y`, an inner `y` shadowing an outer one, an outer parameter read inside an inner lambda, and parameters read after their lambda; `check_zed_scales_linearly_with_lambda_nesting` at 1,000 and 8,000; `the_catalog_floor_texts_hold_for_a_negative_divisor`, which runs the five catalog texts in the interpreter for `a` in -7..7 and `b` in ±1..3 against the floor quotient and remainder); `l01_13_formatter` 24 and `m0_11_prelude_axioms_and_limits` 22 pass. Negative controls, run once and not committed: the old `div_floor` rows fail the divisor test at `a = -7, b = -3`; a set of binders per text passes `xs.map(y -> y).where(w -> w > y)` and fails the scope test; a walk up each name's ancestors fails the nesting test at 34.1 times the work (limit 24). |

| TIME-report | verified, ready for the batch merge on branch wt/time-report (not merged) | COMPLEXITY.md F105. `zedc --report <command> …` runs the command, then writes to standard error a table of its work by stage, unit and definition: instructions retired in user mode (`perf_event_open`, inherited by child processes, so the linker of `zedc build` counts) and bytes allocated (`zed-alloc-count`), with how many times each row was computed and how many times taken from the query cache. `zedc::work` holds the recorder: a thread-local frame stack, each row holding only its own work, so the rows sum to the total exactly; work outside every mark is the row `other`. Marks: `run::load` (load, program), `run::compile` (lex, parse, syntax, resolve, docs, elaborate, linear, and linear per function), `elab::elaborate_in` (check, unit-wide, since the checker checks functions on demand), `testing::checks` (test, per definition, through `counted_row`; a cached row counts as cached at zero work), `run::command_inner` (run), `zed_native::link::build` (lower, codegen, link). The counter is the caller's: `zedc` links `zed-alloc-count` and `perf-event2` in its executable only (dependencies of `zed-native`, named by `main.rs` alone); `nm` finds no `zed_alloc_count` symbol in `libzed_native.a`, so a program `zedc build` makes keeps the system allocator. Where `perf_event_open` is refused, instructions read 0 and the report says why. With no recorder a mark reads one thread-local cell. `zed_tests::scaling::Meter::read` is new, for the tests' counter. Tests: `zedc --lib work::` 6 (exclusive rows and exact sum with a counter the test drives, cached rows, units apart, no recorder, nested recordings, the text form); `l01_30_queries::report` 5 (the rows sum to the total on PRICING and name each stage and definition; a cached definition reports zero after an edit to `tax`, and all rows cached on the same text; 200 rows of one definition show in its own row; scaling through the front end; the recorder alone at 2,000 and 16,000 rows, added by the merge check); `report_binary` 3 (`zedc --report test a.zed b.zed` with equal texts: `b.zed`'s row is `0 0 0 3`; `--report build` has lower, codegen and link rows; a usage error keeps status 2 and reports); `zed_tests` `scaling::tests::a_reading_grows_by_the_work_between`. Scaling: 40 and 320 one-row definitions through the front end and the runner, 7.90 times the instructions, 8.0 times the bytes, limit 24, load 52 to 107. Negative control, run once and not committed: a 10,000,000-step loop in `discount`'s test row raised its row from 242,466 to 120,246,444 instructions while `tax` (243,236 to 246,679) and `total` (168,319 to 170,702) stayed, load 60. Groups run after the rebase on e676cdd2: l01_30 21, l01_21 50, l01_19 28, l01_19a 16, l01_38 235, l01_36a 21, l07_03 19, `zedc --lib` 87, `zed-native --lib` 9, `zed-tests --lib` 40, `zed-kernel --lib scaling::` 23, zed-native binaries 12, all pass, load 25 to 33. Not marked: `--mutate` and `zedc fmt` (their work is in `other`). |

Before, `l01_39_reduced_product::refused`: the test `` `{ g = (x@1, y@1, z@1, w@1) -> if if if ((((((4 * x@1) - (12 * w@1)) … ; g(x@2, y@2, z@2, w@2) }` `` and the constraints `` `12 * w@2 + 10 * y@2 + …` ``; after, `` `g(x, y, z, w)` `` and `` `12 * w + 10 * y + 12 * z <= 4 * x + 11 && …` ``. Tests: that test updated; new `l01_15_kernel_terms::written` (3, one a scaling test), `l01_28_staging::mix::an_unread_local_definition_is_reported_by_its_written_name`, and a renamed-names case in `refused::a_refused_projection_onto_each_name_gives_one_warning_for_each`. | LOAD-chain | merged and pushed in batch 7 (1e9a0245, and the commit that records this) | COMPLEXITY.md LOAD-chain. Cause (perf, `zedc --report`): the linear questions of step L1.38 (`front::linear_questions`) closed each function's body over its cone and the prelude and read it with a fresh transfer (`preimage::relations`), so on a chain of n functions each calling the one before every body read the whole chain again: 270M → 14,602M instructions in the linear stage from 100 to 800 functions on main (54 times). Fix: `preimage::Scoped` numbers the prelude and the unit's definitions once as one chain of lets and reads each body under that scope in a new run that keeps what earlier runs found of the scope (`Run::keep`, as `split_residues` does); what depends on a body (its terms, the scopes its calls bind, the entries keyed by them, summaries, powers: `Fresh`) is dropped after it, in time of what it made. Now 8.2M → 66.0M (8.0 times), and 16.3M → 134.9M from 200 to 1,600 (8.3 times). Tests `l01_38_presburger::one_scope` (3): the relations equal those of the closed bodies in both orders of the bodies, over six units (helpers with `div` and `mod`, bodies outside the domain between bodies inside it, branches, products, chains); the scaling test; the entries held after n bodies, 132 → 657 from 25 to 200 functions. Controls, not committed: main's `front.rs` fails the scaling test (52.0 times the bytes); without the drop the held count grows 61 times and 800 chained tests peak at 927 MB (main 39 MB); dropping every entry a body made fails the scaling test (63.4 times). A chain of tests stays Θ(n²), since its questions hold n²/2 constraints (open point, QUESTIONS.md A35). |

| LOAD-affine-join | merged and pushed in batch 33 (1e4ead23; `crates/zedc-preimage/src/preimage.rs`); no spec change | COMPLEXITY.md LOAD-affine-join. The union of `preimage::identity_classes` is now `preimage::unite`: a refused join is remembered for its two class roots (a refusal is permanent, since a class's functions only grow) and moves with the smaller root when it joins a larger class, and the join's caller and callee are looked up in the other class before the smaller class is read, so a repeated refusal, or one by the join's own functions (`p(x: x, y: x)`), costs O(1). Classes equal the plain union's, root for root (random test, 2000 cases), so no answer changes. Eight pairs of classes refused n/8 times each, n = 1000 → 8000: 7.9 times the bytes; the plain union 37.2 to 57.5 times the instructions (control, not committed). The note's count per pair of roots was not built: O(k²) for one function of k parameters. Open (`// Complexity tradeoff:` at `unite`): first refusals by a third shared function between distinct pairs of classes, up to about n^1.5. |

A link whose largest kept part's point does not extend no longer reads the rest of the conjunction: each kept conjunction has a face, the exact shadow of it onto the atoms its call binds (`extend::Faces`, `Scoped::interface`), with duplicate congruences kept once (`congruences_once`), and the other parts are read through their faces. A face is used only where nothing else reads an atom it projects out; otherwise the old path. The drifting chain (`p{i} = q(x: x) && p{i-1}(x: x + 3) && x < 1000 i`) takes 0.035/0.063/0.129 s at 40/80/160 links (was 0.111/0.415/1.849 s), answers identical; scaling test 8.2 times the bytes at 8n (83.9 before). Disjunctions, negations, `mod`/`div` by a variable, products and let-bound parts keep the old path (`// Complexity tradeoff:` at `Extending::extend`, 0.224/0.910/4.576 s), queued as LOAD-disj-residue |
| LOAD-disj-residue | merged and pushed in batch 41 (7d23ca0c; `crates/zedc-presburger/src/presburger/extend.rs`, `tests/steps/l01_38_presburger/one_scope.rs`); no spec change | COMPLEXITY.md LOAD-disj-residue. A kept part with a disjunction or a negation now has a face too: rows plus clauses, its exact projection onto its caller's atoms (`extend::projected`), each clause kept once by a key that reads a lone quotient's equality as its congruence (`clauses_once`). No residue class is listed, so no face depends on the lcm of the moduli. The chain with `q = (x mod 3 == 1 \|\| x mod 5 == 2) && 0 < x < 1000000` takes 0.066/0.133/0.266 s at 40/80/160 links (was 0.244/1.005/5.399 s), answers identical; scaling test at most 9 times the work, 8.4 times the bytes at 8n. Still O(n²) per chain, as before (`// Complexity tradeoff:` at `Extending::extend`): clauses that never repeat, such as bounds the call moves (`(x < 10 \|\| x > 20)`), and atoms two disjunctions share |
| LOAD-bound-holes | merged and pushed in batch 54 (8980815f; `crates/zedc-presburger/src/presburger/extend.rs`, `extend/holes.rs`, `tests/steps/l01_38_presburger/one_scope.rs`); no spec change | COMPLEXITY.md LOAD-bound-holes. Clauses that say one atom lies outside a hole are kept per face as an ordered map of disjoint intervals (`Holes`, a persistent treap, O(log n) a cut), read through each call's binding; unit equalities form classes rooted at the caller's atom (`unit_classes`), divisions of one term by one modulus read one remainder (`same_divisions`), clauses `d \|\| y <= b` keep the tightest `b` (`rays_once`), and a link whose callees' points all fail first looks for a point of the faces (`Faces::free`). The hole chain takes 0.032/0.098/0.198 s at 40/80/160 links (was 0.39/1.62/7.06), the shared-remainder chain 0.11/0.37 s at 40/80 (was 3.06/25.4). Answers identical to main, except 18 answers in the random-chain tests where `Faces::free` answers and the whole refuses (`Shadows`); each is checked against enumeration over the bounded range. Open: a chain with no point, `(x < 10 \|\| x > 20) && x mod 4 != 1` moved by 3, is decided whole each link and refused, about cubic (24.6 s at 16 links), as on main |
| LOAD-call-relation | merged and pushed in batch 44 (2fc841e1; `crates/zedc-preimage/src/preimage.rs`, `tests/steps/l01_38_presburger/one_scope.rs`); no spec change | COMPLEXITY.md LOAD-call-relation. A call that does not hold its callee's claim no longer reads the callee's body again when the elimination leaves a congruence (`x mod 4 == 1`) or the callee is an integer function over quotients and remainders by constants: the projection keeps those pairs, made once per callee, and writes them at each call as the pairs of the arguments (`Tr::instance`). Congruence chain: 11.8 times the bytes 50 → 400 links (141.1 before); integer chain's relations: 8.1 times (60.2 before). Answers and refusals identical to reading the body again (`Scoped::reading_bodies`; 80 random units). Open: a callee with a disjunction or negation is still read at each call (relations 0.051 → 4.41 s, 50 → 400 links); deciding chains of remainders (`Extending::defined_with`) is above n log n (14.5 s and 47.9 s at 400 links), not touched here; no exact bound on the rows for callees of two or more parameters. |
| LOAD-extend-refuses | merged and pushed in batch 46 (1646684c; `crates/zedc-presburger/src/presburger/extend.rs`, `tests/steps/l01_38_presburger/one_scope.rs`, `tests/steps/l01_38_presburger/extend.rs`); no spec change | COMPLEXITY.md LOAD-extend-refuses. `Extending` no longer refuses a question that `decide` answers on the relation written whole: where the solver refuses the whole form (binding equalities `x@3 == x@4 + 3`, binding-variable constants, one division per call), the question is asked again with those bindings substituted and duplicate divisions merged (`extend::Bound`, exact, time of the formula times a logarithm); the first refusal is kept when both are refused. The chain `x mod 4 != 1 && (x mod 6 == 2 \|\| x mod 10 == 3)` now answers p2 (Ok(true), was Err(Shadows)). Each answer is now checked equal to `decide`'s on the whole wherever that decides, on fixed, random disjunctive and new random negated chains (5 of 10 red on the old code). A question both forms refuse costs two asks (`// Complexity tradeoff:` at `decided`). |

The checker's scopes are lazy (`Scope::lazy`): a spoke, a difference `ai - n >= c` whose leaf `ai` no other conjunct and no written constraint mentions, is left when its hub `n` moves (`Conjuncts::moved`, the `leaves`, `hangs`, `moving` and `pinned` indexes of `Conjuncts`), and the leaf is settled where a read, a test, a join or a binding needs it (`Scope::settled`, `settle_test`, `join`, `bind`); the settled product equals the eager one. Outside `@exact` a difference test is no longer a written constraint (`Scope::atom`). A rejection finds the tests that changed a name by `Arms::lookup` (skew-binary jump pointers, memoized per node and name) in place of `Arms::narrows`. `a_hub_settles_its_scopes_in_time_linear_in_the_tests` un-ignored (0.08 to 0.31 s at `k` = 25 to 400, was 0.29 to 24.1 s at 25 to 200); new `a_hub_tested_between_its_spokes_settles_in_time_linear_in_the_tests` (0.10 to 0.82 s at 25 to 400, was 0.19 to 12.9 s at 25 to 200), `two_hubs_on_one_block_settle_as_the_whole_scope` (answers only; about `k²`, 20.6 s at 384), `l01_39_reduced_product::lazy` (300 random sequences of tests, joins and bindings: every name settled equals the eager product, bottom equal), and a hub unit in `l01_29_diagnostics::batch`. | IS-chain-quad | ready on branch wt/is-chain-quad (not merged); no spec change | COMPLEXITY.md row IS-chain-quad. A chain of k sides joined by `&&` or `\|\|` checked in O(k²): each level narrowed the left side's facts again and copied both sides' facts. A checked condition now carries the narrowing it leaves in place (`Cond::held` in `check/walk.rs`); `&&` takes it over and narrows only its right side's facts on top, `!` flips its side, `if` restores it before its own checks, and fact lists are `VecDeque`s joined shorter onto longer (`check/mod.rs` `joined`), O(k log k) for any nesting. k = 4,000 left-nested: 2.68 s before, 93 ms after. Tests `l01_26_type_resolution::is_chains`: eight correctness tests (long chains, right nesting, `\|\|`, `!` of `\|\|`, a chain of `\|\|` that narrows nothing where it holds, a side far along a chain, a chain outside a condition, the narrowing ending with its branch), each with the same result on the old code; six scaling tests (left, right, mixed left and right, k names, `\|\|`, balanced tree), n = 500 and 4,000; the first five fail on the old code (38.5 to 56.4 times the bytes), negative control not committed |

| LIST-xor-finite | merged and pushed in batch 27 (b47553d5; `crates/zed-kernel/src/types.rs`) | COMPLEXITY.md K69. `Type::each_value` gave `None` for two finite types: `((u, u),)`, `u = 0..8`, met with the exclusive or of `((int - (2k + 1), int),)` for `k < 3`, and the exclusive or of each such row met with the square first. `Type::boxes` gave each tuple type of an expression as a box. It now finds an expression's boxes from its operands' (`part_boxes`, two passes, each operand read once per sign by id, explicit stack): a union has the boxes of its two operands, a meet those of its one operand that has boxes, or, when both have, those of the operand with fewer boxes, each met with the join of the other's unless its columns are all finite; the complement is taken down by De Morgan's laws, so an exclusive or of finite types and a meet with a finite type are finite, and the boxes of an infinite complement are never built. Boxes are kept in persistent sets. Listing: `RowIndex::holds` no longer evaluates the whole part per candidate; the part is a `Graph` of its distinct lists and expressions, a union read by one union merged into it (and so meets), node values kept per class, and a candidate re-reads only the lists of its keyed rows and the nodes whose value changes; a tuple is tried once, in the first box that gives it (`HashSet` keyed by the numbers of its components, `Numbers`, replacing `BoxIndex`), a lookup in the tuple's length whatever the depth of its components. `meet_types` returns `x` for a meet with `top`, so a tuple type of `n` literals is found finite in constant time, not O(n). `Lits` orders by `==` first, which skips shared nodes, so the each-row shape with a side as wide as the rows is found finite in O(n log n) (was O(n²)). Tests: `l01_23_subtyping::listing::finiteness_sees_through_meets_and_exclusive_ors`; `listing::each_value_agrees_with_a_search_of_all_points` (1,500 random types); `listing::a_tuple_is_found_tried_in_the_time_of_its_length`; `scaling::finding_the_boxes_of_a_meet_with_an_exclusive_or_scales`, seven checks of order n (8n within 24 times) on three shapes. `tools/run_tests.sh` runs `types::bdd::tests::exclusive_ors_agree_with_their_values` (35 to 39 s) as a piece of its own. Open: listing the each-row shape at a side of `n` is O(n²) for `n / 2` values (LIST-box-overlap); equal literal sets built apart compare in O(n) (TYPE-hashcons); `((int, 0) \| (0, int)) & ((int, 1) \| (1, int))` is still not known finite (tradeoff comment at `part_boxes`). Negative controls, run once and not committed: finite boxes met with the join, 26.5 times; a union copying both sets, 35.7 times; unmerged union chain, listing 25 times; tried set keyed by values, 101.6 times; `Lits` compared in order only, 35.8 times. | TABLE-life | merged and pushed in batch 18 (2e0df7a3); no spec change | COMPLEXITY.md K66. The per-thread tables of treap nodes, list cells and exclusive ors (CHAIN-zip-size), and `rec.rs` `GROUPS` and `INSTANCES`, kept every entry for the life of the thread, and an expression and its complement held each other: a run of 2,048 independent checks left 167.4 MB allocated on the thread (about 81 KB a check). The tables now hold `Weak` entries that each node removes when dropped, and an expression and its complement hold each other one way only (`Expr::back`), so the same run leaves 0 bytes and the resident bytes stay at 9.5 MB. Ids stay unique and a live node is found again by the same id; a node made again after its drop has a new id and the same digest; a complement made again after its holder was dropped is made from the form it was written with (`Expr::source`): same structure and digest, so order and printed text do not depend on what was freed. Rows are kept in a weak table (`ROWS`), so equal rows alive together are one node. Tests `l01_22_type_representation::table_life` (bounded memory at n and 8n, scaling of a run by its length), `types::bdd::tests` (three lifetime tests), `types::rec::tests` (two). The meet scaling test of `types::bdd::tests` is unchanged: it had found its second list's rows again only through the nodes the immortal tables kept, and now finds them through `ROWS`. Open: a recursive instance is a strong cycle through its members' keys and unfoldings and is never freed (item REC-cycle); `Key::group` (from main) names a group by the address of its live `Env`, which is unique only while that instance lives. |

A due tree with neither, outside a whole-scope projection, is narrowed by `Scope::shortest`: the scope keeps a potential, repaired for the new edges by the Cotton–Maler 2006 Dijkstra on reduced costs (a negative cycle empties the scope), then the bounds move by Dijkstra from the changed names, O((names changed + their edges) log n) per test. The potential is repaired at every edge added whichever way its tree is projected (`Scope::repair`, review 2026-11-07), so it holds at every edge but those of names it lacks; the soundness argument for checking each edge as read is in the doc of `Scope::repair`. Any other tree or a name with no interval falls back to the projection; the potential is set again from `presburger::satisfiable` only once per tree after a projection of every block (tradeoff comment at `Scope::reseed`; k `if`s each after an `if` on a sum, between rungs, 8.4 times the bytes at 40 and 320, no reseed). The same mix on a hole is 58.3 times, as at the base: pre-existing, in the projection of the holed tree. The ladder of k `if` statements is now order 1 in both orders (12.4 times the bytes at 40 and 320; 51.8 times with the new path off). Every reduce of the l01_39*, l01_38, l01_25 and zedc_preimage groups gave the same scope both ways (361 pieces, 0 mismatches, a temporary cross-check not committed). The search for a value that `Type::verdict` runs before a refusal (`Searching::find_rep`) tried one candidate for each tuple type of a tuple part, narrowing the box into it; each narrowing tested every open cell of the components it changed, so n candidates cost n by n new types, order n^2 on the Fork family (pairs of integer lists less, for each i, those whose first list holds only i or something else, with a second list of the parity of i). A candidate inside a tuple type the part excludes (`Part::forced`, computed once) holds no value of the part, so it is now skipped; the answer for that candidate was already none. `refusals::a_fork_refusal_is_reached_in_the_size_of_the_pairs`: n 8 and 64, 8.5 times the instructions, 8.0 times the bytes, Refused(Fork) at each size; control (no skip, run once, not committed): 50.4 times the instructions, 46.8 times the bytes, fails. Check run once, not committed: with every skipped candidate narrowed anyway and an assertion that none found a value, the groups l01_22, l01_23, l01_24, l01_26, l01_26a, l01_27, l01_29, zed_kernel and zedc pass but for the new scaling test. Open: a part whose candidates are not excluded and none holds a value still costs n narrowings of n cells. |

| PROV-union-growing | merged and pushed in batch 60 (94b14f92); no spec change | `Provenance::origins` on a union chain whose entries each hold a set one failure larger than the last (COMPLEXITY.md K25, fixed). Each set records the set it was made from and the failures it adds, and a family's tally counts a set on top of a counted base, at the cost of what it adds; the first set counted in an empty tally is counted whole, so a family of one entry costs `O(log n)`. `origins` finds the sets oldest fact first, over the facts `i` reaches, so each family's set is found from the set it was made from and shares its nodes, and the meet of the result's set with each `let` binding is found from the last meet by their difference. `n` = 400 to 6400: 16.5, 36.4, 81.1, 201.7, 411.8 ms (main: 99.8 ms at 400, 1705 ms at 1600). Tests in `m0_09_kernel_evaluator::provenance`: scaling at `n` = 400 and 3200 (old code: fails, 49.4 times the bytes, limit 24); a table of growing entries and a table whose first entry grows, against the search per member; and `provenance::tests` in `zed-kernel`, the tally when the set counted whole leaves or is replaced. `// Complexity tradeoff:` in `origins`: the oldest-first pass reads every reached fact's value, so a failure read beside a large value holding none costs that value's parts, at most the run's work (64,000 entries: 22 ms, against 0.01 ms before and a 340 ms run) |
| PROV-oldest-failing | merged and pushed in batch 65 (40733fae, e35d2631); no spec change | COMPLEXITY.md K25. Left by PROV-union-growing: the oldest-first pass of `Provenance::origins` read every fact `i` reaches, so a failure read beside a large value holding none cost that value's parts. Each fact now records, when the run makes it, whether its value is a failure or holds one in a tuple, a keypath or a family's value (`Fact::holds_failure`), from a per-run memo `Holds` in `provenance.rs` kept in the evaluator's `Trace`: each non-empty sequence and family is read once by its id, a family made from another by `union` or `Family::extend`, which keep every entry, holds a failure when that family does and is otherwise read by their shallow difference (`Family::shallow_diff`, now with two lifetimes), and the value of a union the run evaluates is not read at all: it holds a failure exactly when one of its operands does (`Holds::set`). `Family::extend` checks in debug builds that no entry is replaced by another value. `origins` reaches only facts whose flag is set and skips unset inputs in its backward pass; a fact whose value holds no failure holds no member, so every origin set is unchanged. A table of 64,000 rows beside `#late`: origins 0.02 ms (88 ms without the flag, load 30-40). Recording cost in instructions (`scaling::Meter`, one `trace` per program, without `Holds` meaning the flag set on every fact; best of 2, the runs within 0.01%): that table 1,234.3 M → 1,278.5 M (+3.6%), `growing_entries(3200)` 321.7 M → 329.2 M (+2.3%), the same 3200-entry table with no failure 258.0 M → 266.9 M (+3.4%), 20,000 missing readings 779.6 M → 785.9 M (+0.8%), deep replacements 32 steps at depth 192 20.4 M → 26.9 M (+32%: its 6,144 literal one-entry families are each read once, about 1,050 instructions each). The first version cost 1,277.8 M, 374.1 M (+16%) and 40.9 M (+100%) on three of these: it kept a count per family, hashed ids with SipHash, and diffed each union of a 3200-entry table against its operand, about 12,000 instructions for a one-item difference because `imbl` 7.0.2's diff visits the boundary nodes of each shared subtree (perf with frame-pointer call graphs: `Holds::of` 10.1% of `growing_entries`, mostly the diff cursor). Now ids hash by `types::KeyHasher`, a family keeps only whether it holds a failure, and union values are not read. Tests: scaling `the_origins_of_a_failure_read_beside_a_table_holding_none_cost_nothing_for_the_table` (`n` = 8000 and 64000, power 0: 1.0 times the work and bytes; with the flag ignored in `origins`, run once and not committed, it fails on 8.0 times the bytes, limit 3); `a_table_whose_rows_hold_failures_in_some_entries_agrees_with_a_search`; `provenance::tests::holds_agrees_with_a_tree_read_over_values_and_families_made_from_others` in `zed-kernel` (now also over families made from a failure-free one and nested unions). `// Complexity tradeoff:` in `origins`: a failure read beside a large value holding other failures still costs that value's parts |

| CLASS-atoms | merged and pushed in batch 62 (55045d49); no spec change | COMPLEXITY.md F117 (replaces F74's alias union-find). The author's Q5 (2026-11-07): type inference runs after bubbling, so an undeclared parameter takes every loose atom that passes by rule 6 out of a call or operator applied to it and reaches an `else`, else-pattern, `is`, `== #a` or `!= #a` that handles it, also through a helper and through names bound to computed values. Before, only a handler whose operand was the parameter's name, or a name bound to it (`s = line`), counted: `s = trim(line); s else ""` handled nothing for `line`, and `f(#nf)` passed the atom by instead of running the `else`. Now the resolver gives each handler a region and records each name use whose atoms reach it (`Resolution::regions`, `Resolution::flows`, replacing `handles` and `aliases`; `Resolver::flow`): the arguments and receiver of a call, the family `m` of `m[k]`, the operands of operators other than `==`/`!=`, `if` branches, a block's result and string holes, at any depth; an inner `else` is a region that passes on to the outer one, and `s = e` is a region that passes the atoms of `s`'s uses into `e`'s names, not back (`{ s = a + b; a else 0 }` handles nothing for `b`). `check::handled` joins them by an iterative Tarjan over binders and regions. The key `k` of `m[k]` is left out: with it, `r(fs, args, k) = fs[args[k] else ""]` gave `k` the atoms and the call check rejected `main` ("`r` applies, does not take #_"), 6 tests of `l01_26_type_resolution::given` failed; the same rejection of `{ d = k else 0; return fs[args[k] else ""]; }` is present on main 9f176f5d and is not fixed here (CLASS-atoms-index-key, open). Also (F18): a generic call whose every argument passed by now gives the empty type, not a dynamic one (`walk.rs`, `apply_one`), so `f(#a, #b)` with `f(x, y) = x + y` gives the atoms. Printed signatures: `f(positional x) = trim(x) else "dflt"` is `(String \| #_) -> String`, `trim(x) else #nf -> "dflt"` is `(String \| #nf \| #nf(..)) -> String`. Tests (`l01_26_type_resolution::handled`, 27): the old test that a computed value handles nothing is replaced by `a_name_bound_to_a_computed_value_takes_what_its_handler_handles`; new `a_binding_passes_atoms_from_its_name_to_its_value_and_not_back`, `a_handler_on_a_computed_value_prints_its_atoms_on_the_parameter_only` (expected printed signatures), `an_inferred_parameter_and_its_declared_twin_give_the_same_value_at_each_call` (table: each handler form and a helper, three calls each including an atom no handler names), `an_argument_whose_atoms_a_parameter_takes_in_part_passes_the_rest_by` (F17, F27; both already ran on main), `a_generic_call_whose_arguments_are_atoms_only_passes_them_by` (F18); scaling `atom_scaling::a_parameter_trimmed_through_a_chain_of_names_scales` and `a_parameter_inside_many_nested_handlers_scales`. Red before green: 5 of the 27 fail on main's checker. Affected groups pass: l01_26 283 (1 ignored), l01_16, l01_27 48, zedc-check, zedc-resolve (30 pieces, 0 failed). Repro rerun (narrowing/, differential/, weak-tests/, orchestrator/): now agree with the declared twin or run: cata, eqLa, lam3a, nea, numeqa, orda, outer2a, outera, qda, rs1, teq, touter, tqd, twoa, w01, w02, w04, w05, w07, eq, eq2, F18, z15. Still differ, open: propa and propla (delegation `f(x) = g(x)` with no handler, F3: no handler, so no atoms, by the rule as written); tup2a (an atom in a tuple component, struct positions, L2.6b); lita (`x == 3`, a non-atom, does not handle); differential/l02 (a row lambda in a test row is rejected, unrelated); `if x == #nf {..} else { trim(x) }` gives `String \| #nf`, since `==` does not narrow, as for a declared parameter (eqb2, tup2b2); f18b, `(#a, #b)` as one argument, gives `#errs({#a, #b})`, accepted. DOCS-TODO (spec repo, not edited here): a contrasting example of a handler on a computed value, `s = trim(line); s else ""` against `{ s = a + b; a else 0 }`. |
| EMPTY-level | merged and pushed in batch 64 (b89a0700, 6ad99bb2, ea5b4452); spec unchanged (DESIGN M0.9 (d), (e), sec:remap_law already say it); DOCS-TODO entry 20, zed c45036d | Author, Q4, 2026-11-07. An empty family as a leaf is an empty level: `Family::from_leaves` keeps it, `leaves` gives it back as a short keypath, and in `Levels` it merges with any level and collides only with a leaf. So `discard` keeps the key of a level it empties, `{"a" -> {}} >< max` is `{"a" -> #empty}`, `.count` gives `{"a" -> 0, ...}`, and `{"a" -> {}}` differs from `{}`. A reindex calls its key function on a short keypath with the kernel's own atom `#unbound key` (a name no Zed program can write) for the value and for `pathKey` past the end of that keypath: an output keypath that reads none of them keeps the key (cut at the first component holding it, so `into (k.upper -> j -> v)` gives `{"A" -> {}}` and rank over the outer level ranks it); one that reads them first drops it (transpose, flatten, group by the value). A key that is `#notFound` or another atom held as data is kept, at a full keypath and above an empty level: `Prim::Path` holds atoms as data, as a tuple does, and no longer merges them into one failure key, so the identity reindex of `{#red -> {1 -> 5}}` is itself (on main it gave `{#red -> 5}`). The `Lines` writers (`zed_rt::ports::write`, `zedc::run::join_lines`) write no line for an empty level, the bytes main wrote; the checker already rejects a push that is not a string or a family of strings, so only a `_` value reaches this. Also fixed: `fold` and `next_group` recursed into each other once per level needing no call (COMPLEXITY.md K78). Tests that encoded the old dropping changed: m0_09 `discard_drops_out_of_stock_items_and_keeps_their_emptied_category`, `fam_builds_a_table_over_a_domain`, the scaling model of `a_family_built_with_families_as_leaves_agrees_with_its_leaves`; m0_10 law 1 (renamed) and the sparse-relation helpers of laws 15 and 19 (`prune`). The transpose warning is EMPTY-level-swap |

Feeds L3.2. `==` on `Ty`, `Row`, `Shape` and `Sig` was derived and recursed once per level, so two equal types built apart overflowed the stack (100,000 levels on 2 MiB, 200,000 tuples on 8 MiB) and two types of `n` shared nodes and `2^n` paths each took `2^n` time. Follow-ups of MINMAX-stack. A reordered signature (`fit_declared`) keeps `Sig::word`; a partial application keeps none, as `min` and `max` take two tuples only when both arguments are given (sec:reduction, item 5). `Ck::repairs` finds prelude words by `Sig::word`, not by name and signature equality. A parameter whose values meet a tuple in `min` or `max` was narrowed to numbers; its class now keeps its non-number carriers, as `<` does, and records a `NeedKind::Order` need that each value flowing in and each call re-checks, so `g(a) = min(a, (1, 2))` takes `(0, 5)` and refuses `(0.5, 1.0)`, `3` and tuples of another length with `min`'s own messages. A generic function `g(a, b) = min(a, b)` still takes numbers only (`(T, T) -> T for T in int \| f64 \| rat`), the same as `<`. Messages about `min` reached through a function value `f` read "`f`, which is `min`, does not take ...". The needs of one class at one `min` or `max` site are one need, its other operands joined one shape per length (sec:reduction, item 5), and where one operand is a class and nothing else, the result is a variable a call binds to the join of the operands, so `g(a) = min(a, (1, 2))` prints `(_) -> _` and `g((0, 5))` is `(0 | 1, 2 | 5)`; a `min` of that result joins into the same use. `if` results stay unions. The literal chain `g(i)(a) = g(i - 1)(min(a, (i, 0)))` costs 8.2 times at 40/320 links (61.5 before) and grows 2.02 to 2.30 times per doubling up to 1,280. `min(2.0, 1.0 / 0.0)` is `2.0`; `f64(#inf)` still evaluates to the atom `#inf`, which is plan row NAN-cast-atoms, not `min`. |

| BIGINT-fast-mul | merged and pushed in batch 66 (4792132d, 1d222ff3; `crates/zed-kernel/src/num.rs`, `num/fast.rs`, `crates/zedc-int/src/int.rs`, `crates/zedc-presburger/src/presburger/deep/canon.rs`, tests in `l01_17_value_representation::subquadratic`); no spec change | COMPLEXITY.md K82 (K81 at first; L01_24-decimal-scale took K81 in batch 65) and section "Crossovers", BIGINT-fast-mul; ZED_PORT_GAPS.md "Entries from BIGINT-fast-mul". Every product of two big integers in `zed-kernel/src/num.rs`, `zedc-int/src/int.rs` and `zedc-presburger` `deep/canon.rs` now calls `zed_kernel::num::mul_uint`, `mul_int` or `pow_uint` (`num::fast::mul`, `mul_int`, `pow`) instead of num-bigint 0.4.8's `*`, which is Toom-3 above 256 words, O(n^1.465), with no transform. `fast::mul` gives a product whose narrower factor has at most 512 words to num-bigint, and above that takes the least of three computed costs (num-bigint's `TOOM_COST l s^0.465`, rows over nonzero words, the three-prime transform plan); all three give the same product. The transform was made 1.5 to 2 times faster (no 128-bit division in the reductions, Garner's constants at compile time, root-1 stages without products): 12.2 ns per `n log2(n)`, `TRANSFORM_COST` 20 to 12. Measured 2026-11-07, load 16 to 41: `mul` over num-bigint 0.86 at 2048 words, 0.65 at 4096, 0.49 at 8192, 0.38 at 16384, 0.44 at 8192 by 65536; 1.00 where num-bigint is taken (to 1536 and at 2560); at 3072 by 3072 the costs are within 5% and the time is bimodal, 1.01 or 1.38 (cause not measured). Stay on num-bigint: products by a machine word, `modpow` (num-bigint's Montgomery ladder) and `zed/corpus/ref` (may not depend on the compiler; its P14 factors lie below 512 words). Tests: `l01_17_value_representation::subquadratic::products_agree_with_num_bigint_across_the_crossovers` (416 products of random, all-ones, power-of-two and one-word factors in both orders, signs, zero, powers) and `a_dense_product_scales` (8192 to 65536 words: 9.5 times in instructions, limit 24; Toom-3 would be 21). Negative control, not committed: Garner's carry without `t >> 64`; the unit tests `num::fast::tests::products_agree_with_num_bigint`, `gcds_agree_with_num_bigint` and `decimals_agree_with_num_bigint` fail and `quotients_agree_with_num_bigint` does not end (Newton's iteration on wrong products; stopped after an hour); `products_agree_with_num_bigint_across_the_crossovers` fails at its first shape above the leaf, 513 by 70000 words. Memory asks unchanged: `mul_int` peaked at 5.00 times the product's width, `pow_uint` at 6.43 times the power's, below the eight times `room` asks |
| PARSE-one-tuple | merged and pushed in batch 67 (35247d7c; `crates/zedc-syntax/src/format.rs`, tests in `l01_10_lambdas_types_else_tuples::one_tuples`; the surface `match` test waits for L2.4, written into its plan note); no spec change (the grammar took `(e,)` and `(p,)` in f77d6f34 and zed 23ade74; DOCS-TODO entry 8 already states the rule) | COMPLEXITY.md F121. The parser and AST builder already read `(e,)` as `Tuple([e])` and `(p,)` as a one-component tuple pattern, and the kernel, type and value printers wrote `,)` (VALUE-one-tuple); the source formatter did not: it wrote `(1,)` as `(1)`, which reads back as `1`, and the pattern `(a,)` as `(a)`, which does not parse. `format.rs` now closes a tuple of one component, expression or pattern, with `,)`. Tests in `l01_10_lambdas_types_else_tuples::one_tuples`: `(1,)`, `((1,),)`, `((1),)` and the arms `(a,)`, `((a,),)`, `(_,)` parse; `(1,)`, `((1,),)` evaluate to `(1,)` and `((1,),)`, `(1)` to `1`; the arm `(a,) -> a` binds the component of `(5,)` and passes `(5, 6)` by, run as the kernel arm it elaborates to; each form prints back as written; `(,)`, `(1,,)`, the pattern `(a)` and `((a),)` are rejected, `(a)` at its `)` with `','` expected; a one-tuple nested n deep scales (F121). Not done: the surface `match v { (a,) -> a }` is elaborated from step L2.4 ("a `match` is compiled from step L2.4"), so its evaluation is the ignored test `a_surface_match_on_a_one_tuple_evaluates`, which L2.4 should un-ignore. Red before the fix: 4 of the 5 first tests failed on the formatter's `(1)`. Groups run, before and after: l01_08 (18 pieces), l01_10, l01_13, l01_22, l01_26, l02_01, l02_02, l02_03, l03_01, l06_01, l07_03, zedc, zedc_syntax, deep_types: 33 pieces, 0 failed both times; l01_10 14 passed before, 20 passed and 1 ignored after |

Follow-ups RUNG2-definedness (D9, stacked on this branch), RUNG2-carrier (D6), C2-chain (one chain of premises connecting all factors), LADDER-tie-rung5 (D3). |
| LIST-size | merged and pushed in batch 68 (623a07d2; spec d3cca4b with DOCS-TODO entry 28); no grammar change | COMPLEXITY.md C5, C18. Author's answer to Q11 A: enumerate only domains whose members the source lists, never a range, so no width changes the method. `store::preimage::enumerate` and `preimage_ref::enumerate` lose the width rule (`hi - lo <= 1` per piece) and take the listed names (`Listed`, passed by `preimage_listed`; `preimage` lists none), since integer sets merge adjacent members and a list cannot be read from its pieces; the one-multi-member-input rule stays. `store::product::reduce_domains` lists an interval into the type only when it has one member (it listed one of at most `2 * pieces` members), and `atom_names` passes the names `listed` finds. Lost: a range of one or two members a piece is no longer decided member by member (e.g. `n >= 0 && n <= 3` then `n != 2` keeps `int[0..=1] \| int[3..=3]` with no finite set); `n == 0 \|\| … \|\| n == 3` keeps `{0, 1, 3}`. L2.6's overlap check is not built, so only the spec sentence (rule 6) changed. Baseline group (l01_24..29, l01_38, l01_39*, m0_05, zedc*): 401 pieces, 1775 passed (1 piece timed out, pre-existing slow) before; 401 pieces, 1779 passed, 0 failed, 4 ignored after. Two known-limit tests for two holes that together leave out a value (CE-B and the collective two-hole example, XST-r2 META2): a value the two holes leave out together is kept in the scope though no solution has it, checked sound against the interpreter at every point of the bounds in three orders of the tests; exactness is left to ST-holes-moves. l01_39 by module: 196 passed, 1 ignored, 0 failed (baseline 194/1/0). |

## Decisions made

- Toolchain: Fedora's rustc and cargo 1.99.1, edition 2024. CI uses the stable toolchain.
- Cargo's target directory is `build/cargo`, set in `.cargo/config.toml`.
- Language tests: `tests/steps/<step>/`, all in one test binary. Unit tests of internal
  functions: `#[cfg(test)]` beside the code.
- Fences: CI must not read the spec repository (the author's instruction, 2026-10-29).
  The `sync` tool, run by hand, copies the fences into `zed/fences/design.md` and
  `examples.md`, each under a header with its source line and layer; `sync --check` exits
  1 when a copy is stale, and the step test `l01_07_fences::copies::the_copies_match_the_spec`
  compares the texts.
- Spec location for tests (TEST-spec-path): the tests that read the spec repository
  (`l01_07_fences::copies::the_copies_match_the_spec`, `l01_08_parser::cases::the_copies_match_the_spec`,
