The binder of a type parameter or of a pattern field `f:` is the whole declaration or pattern, as for every binder (`Binder::span`), since the AST keeps no span per parameter or field. A field written twice in a struct is rejected by the AST builder (L2.1), before resolution. A `match` reported as a later form does not report its arms' patterns again, and the ends of a range pattern are part of the range (the `l03_01_parser::later` expectations). `zedc run` reads a package only when the unit has an import or the manifest names a prelude, so a single file under a manifest runs as before. The tests `l01_11_statements` (`local` in a block now builds), `l01_14_name_resolution`, `l01_29_diagnostics::binder_type`, `l02_01_parser_structs::later`, `l02_02_parser_match_imports::later` and the step index snapshot of `m0_01_scaffolding` changed; the snapshot also gains `l01_19a_command_line` and `l01_39b_on_demand`, which were missing from it at a9330ae7. The resolution key holds the number the database gives each import's exports, not a BLAKE3 digest of them, since L1.37a keeps hashes out of keys (repair 1, at the rebase onto 0b7ff697). The scaling test of many empty roots is the one a linear scan in `unit::add` fails (47.2 times the instructions); the other five pass with it.
- LR4-fix: LR4 was merged after a rebase onto L2.1 without a test run; at its own base no display read `Expr12`'s node, so its tests passed. Any builder that tells kids apart by rule name over a position where `Expr12` (or another `~` rule) stands has the same hazard; `display` was the only one found (the other rule-name tests in `ast` read `Update`, `PlainArg`, `Item`, `Params`, `ArrowParams`, `Type` and `FieldPat`, none of which a pass alternative yields). Negative control: the old rule-name test put back fails 7 tests of `l02_01_parser_structs`, the 3 reported and 4 of the 5 new ones (the fifth checks the refusal of a field given twice, which both pass).
- L3.1: `not (a in b)` is a syntax error, since Zed negates with `!` (`!(a in b)`); `a in b in c` is a syntax error, since `InTest` does not chain. `(..<)` is a syntax error: a range operator is not a `BinOp`. Nested parentheses around a section build one section, `((+))` is `(+)`. `[int]` in an expression is an array display of the name `int`, and a type only in type position. `{ x }` is a set and `{}` the empty map, as Display precedes Block in Primary. A `for` body must export: `for x in m { g(x); }` stops at its expression statement (L6). The formatter writes a `for` in parentheses as the operand of an operator and as a `for` source or guard, which the grammar does not need; `if` and `match` operands are still written bare (`if a { 1 } else { 2 } + 1`), as before. With the forms of L3 building, ten more corpus programs build and format to a fixed point, and each differs from its text only by hand layout the formatter does not do (`=` and trailing comments aligned, long lines wrapped, a `match` on one line in `23-join-zoo.zed`), listed in `l01_13_formatter::fences`; the corpus or the formatter must change for the test to compare them. Tests changed: `l01_08_parser::deep` (a spread suffix, L6, in place of a display at the bottom of a deep nest), `l01_09_postfix_chains::calls` (`unordered`, L9, in place of `::len`), `l01_11_statements::blocks` (`({ x })` is now a set), `l01_14_name_resolution::fences` (accepts " is compiled from step L3." and later-layer annotations, since fences using L3 forms now build), `l02_01_parser_structs::blocks` (sets and maps build), the step index snapshot of `m0_01_scaffolding`.
- L6.1: The grammar has no labeled `break`: a loop's name names its step index, and `break` ends the innermost loop, so `break i` in an inner loop is a break with the outer step as its value. `p'.x = 1;` and `p.x' = 1;` parse as method declarations (`T.m = e`); resolution refuses them as primed names (L6.3). The lexer takes one prime per name: `x''` is a lexical error. `x = 1 with;`, `x[] = 0;` and `break y -> y` are syntax errors. The statement order of a block is normalized in the builder as well as the formatter, so a text and its formatted text build to the same statements; the s-expression of a block lists `while` guards first and `until` guards last. A block that ends in an expression statement exports nothing, so `for x in m { g(x); }` and `x -> { y = x; y; }` now fail with "this block exports nothing" where they failed with "this form (ExprStmt) is built from layer L6". `a*.5` lexes `*.` as one token and is a syntax error ("expected IDENT"); `a * 0.5` is the product. With `loop` and indexed definitions building, nine more corpus programs build and format to a fixed point, each differing from its text only by hand layout, listed in `l01_13_formatter::fences`; three of them (15, 24, 28) write a `while` guard after the step, where DESIGN line 428 has the formatter write it first, so the corpus or that rule should change. Tests changed: `l01_08_parser::deep` (`unordered`, L9, in place of a spread suffix), `l01_09_postfix_chains::calls` (the spread case dropped), `l01_11_statements::blocks` (the exports-nothing error; a keyed push, L7, in place of a boundary), `l03_01_parser::later` (the exports-nothing error), `l01_13_formatter::fences` (the nine programs), `l01_14_name_resolution::fences` (accepts " is compiled from step L6."), the step index snapshot of `m0_01_scaffolding`.
- L25.8: The brief says "no other skipping" than the optimizer-equal mutants. A mutant can turn a program whose rows end into one that never ends: `w(positional f) = f(f); guard(positional x) = if x > 0 { w(w) } else { x } test { 0 -> 0 }` ends, and its mutant with `0` replaced by `-1` takes the `w(w)` branch, so `zedc test` on the mutated text did not end in 20 s (killed, exit 124). With no recursion at L1 the only source of such a loop is self-application, but a runner without a budget (docs/thresholds.md) cannot stop it. So a mutant's rows run only when `stage::ends` admits each closed term, and a mutant with a row it does not admit is counted "not run" and printed with its position. The cost: a higher-order call through a parameter (`twice(f: inc, x)`) is not admitted, so its definition's mutants are not run. The front end, the mixer and term equality recurse, so a chain of 800 additions needs the 8 MiB main stack (`zedc` runs on it; the scaling test uses `on_main_sized_stack`); `mutate`'s own walks use explicit stacks. `Info.quotients` records each `*` whose product the checker types with no `int` (`rat` or `f64`), where `/` is defined; `*` on `int` has no `/` mutant. Tests changed, since every step's `zed/` unit is run by the L1 harnesses and the new `fares.zed` adds 7 checks: `l01_33_lowering::corpus` (53 rows, 65 lowered terms), `l01_34_memory::corpus` and `l01_36_cranelift::corpus` (53 rows; 65 terms, 117 cells, 121 or 147 writes), `l01_37_oracle::every_step_unit_agrees` (130 native runs, 14 compared), the usage line in `l01_21_test_runner::command`, and the step index snapshot of `m0_01_scaffolding`. `tools/run_tests.sh 4`: 98 pieces, 1 failed, `l01_29_diagnostics::printed::every_printed_type_parses_back_to_itself` (25 `#ioError(_)` types of L1 units, failing on main since 4d82672, item L29-print).
- OSP-lit: read failures now carry `#ioError(op, path, why)`, replacing the bare `#ioError` of the L1.19 note above; the types still print the atom as `#ioError(_)` at L1. The path of a `Lines` port's error is the port's name. A bare pattern `#a` in `else` matches `#a` with any payload or none (DESIGN L1.27 allows it). A doc link resolves against every binder of the unit and the prelude, not by scope. An unattached doc block is an error, since the checker has no warnings. A float literal is limited by its rounded value only; the cost of reading the digits of a long int or rat literal is that of `num-bigint`'s decimal parse, which is quadratic in the digits (not changed here).
- OSP-drop: the statement-position parts (a statement `e else d;` and `*.`) are left to L6.3. The match arm `_ ->` is a pattern, not a binding (DESIGN.md's table of `_`), and `match` arrives at L2.4, so the test checks only that it is refused as L2.4 and not as a drop. `(_, x) = …` is not a binding at L1 (`TupleParam` is L2), so nested `_` is tested as the target list `_, x = …`. The repair note writes `_ = … else #ok;` and does not quote the value, since the checker has no source text and `Expr`'s display is prefix form. A default that is not an atom literal (a name, a call) names no atoms: its own loose atoms are walked like any other value's.
- OSP-drop2: a name bound by `name = e` now carries the naming of `e`: `y = lookup(1) else #notFound; _ = y;` is accepted, where OSP-drop rejected it. The set kept per expression may hold more atoms than its type, where a part's failure stops there (`==` gives a `bool`); `_` keeps only those of the type it binds, so the extra atoms can only reject. `Ty::dropped` is no longer used by the checker, only by `discard.rs`. The match criterion is written as an ignored test, since resolve refuses `match` until L2.4. `l01_29_diagnostics::printed::every_printed_type_parses_back_to_itself` fails on this branch and on its base b9115b2 alike (25 printed types such as `#ioError(_)` hold a form of layer L2); it is not caused here.
- L1.19a: DESIGN did not say how a `main` test row gives a `CommandLine` port; it is a `String` with one argument per line, split as a `Lines` input is, so an argument holding a newline cannot be given by a row (the spec sentence is on `wt/l1-19a-spec`). Words of other `zedc` commands that are not UTF-8 are usage errors too, since `main.rs` now reads `args_os`. The record without its program's content address, noted here, was fixed by L1.19b.
- L1.19b: DESIGN (step L1.19) says a record names "the content address of its program" without saying what is hashed. The implementation hashes the canonical address of the term a wave runs (`main` closed over the definitions it reaches, under the prelude) and the ports' text, so two programs that differ only in local names or layout share one address, and a change to a function `main` does not reach keeps it. The address is taken once per program at its first wave, O(t log t), also when nothing is recorded. Settled 2026-11-05 by the orchestrator: the address covers the term `main` runs and the port declarations, since a function `main` does not reach cannot change a run; DESIGN step L1.19 says so on `wt/l1-19b-spec`.
- CHK-cost (with CHK-curried and CHK-carrier; COMPLEXITY F77): nested tuples n^3 -> linear (39k instructions per node), nested lambdas n^2 -> linear (62k per node), `+` 1.1M -> 140k instructions per node, negation 390k -> 65k, call 340k -> 80k. Type variables keep their class name until `finish` writes the names once, so curried lambdas using every parameter are linear (8.4 times the instructions, 8.2 times the bytes at n = 100 and 800). A class solved to a carrier is replaced outside the lambdas only and read through the union-find inside them (`Ck::stale`): curried lambdas whose parameters take a carrier 8.0 times the instructions and 7.9 times the bytes (n^2 before), test no longer ignored; a chain of calls `g(0)(1)…(n)` is linear by a memoized `Sig::digest`. Repair 5: `is` tests compare such lambdas through the resolver (`Ty::diff_with`); before, the branch where a tuple holding one is not of the tested type kept it, in programs already rejected because a tuple takes no function. `curried_classes` checks 25 programs against the output of the build that replaces classes at every depth. Open: a held function at the bottom of n nested tuples is n^2 in its error notes (ledger CHK-held-cascade).
- CHK-held-cascade (COMPLEXITY F89): the error for a function held in a tuple is now given once, at the tuple expression that holds it (`Ty::unchecked_function`), not again where the value flows through more tuples, bindings or calls; this changes which span the error names when a tuple is made inside a called function from its argument (the call that binds it, `wrap(scale)`). Left for the author, as each is a choice of what a message shows: n separate tuple expressions holding one function are n real errors whose derivations repeat O(n) notes each, O(n^2) in all; one error through n nested tuples of data has 2n notes each printing a subtuple, O(n^2) text; a type that shares a row at each level prints in 2^n characters. The whole check of a type 2,000 tuples deep still overflows a 2 MiB stack in `zed_kernel::types::empty::Search::rep` (T2-types-rest); `Ty::kernel`, `replace_in` and `subst` still recurse once a level (T2-ty).
- CHK-need-uses: the destructuring need is a tuple of exactly `n`, as the run's `TupleGet` needs, so a longer tuple is rejected rather than read by its first `n` components. The rejection has no row in `zed/fences/rejections.txt`, as the tuple-index need has none. A need that names a variable the call leaves unbound (a generic function passed as an argument) was measured at the merge and was quadratic twice over: `check::bound` copied the call's binds at each such need, and `Ty::replace_in` scanned every bound variable at each signature it met (that scan was on main before this item); both are fixed (COMPLEXITY F90). Open, not measured: when a signature does hold a bound variable, `Ty::replace_in` still builds the binds that are not its own by a pass over all of them.
- CHK-index-else: `r(fs, args, k) = fs[args[k]]`, with no `else`, is still rejected with "this function, which `r` applies, does not take #notFound, and no argument of this call gives it". Rule 6 passes an atom by when an argument of the call gives it; `args` is a `CommandLine`, whose reads give `#notFound`, but the checker counts only the atoms at the top of an argument's type, not those its reads give. Whether rule 6 counts the atoms of what a function-valued argument gives is an author question. Also, `g(r) = r else 0` keeps its parameter's variable whole (`(r: _) -> 0 | _`), so `g(r: args[1])` still holds `#notFound`: an over-approximation for a parameter that may hold any value, left as it was.
- ROW-made-cost (COMPLEXITY F93): `Row::made` merged two mostly overlapping variable sets by inserting each variable of the smaller, O(n^2 log n) over n rows `t(k) = (t(k-1), (t(k-1), Vk))` (70.6 times the instructions at 250 and 2,000); `check::ty::merge` now steps `imbl::OrdSet::diff` over the shared nodes in turn with the insertions, 10.0 times. Open, author question A8: rows merging two sets that share no node (`z(k) = (x(k), y(k))`) stay O(n^2 log n), test under `#[ignore]` naming A8; the rest of the item starts fresh from main once A8 is answered. Found: `Ty::replace_in` walks a shared row once per path, 2^n on `p(s, v) = (s, (s, v))` nested n deep (ledger REPLACE-shared-rows).
- ATOM-payload-fn (COMPLEXITY F95): an atom whose payload holds a function is refused by `==`, `!=` and `String` and as a tuple component, at any depth of payloads; the refusal of `==` now has the derivation of its operand, so `==` and `String` messages that had no notes now have some. Open: `held_wide` on wt/atom-payload-sub needs the same walk; `Row::open` ignores payload variables, so a payload built from a parameter and returned in an export tuple escapes the check (ATOM-payload-open); `#a(#a(…))` n deep is O(n^2), pre-existing; fixed by ATOM-nested-sq (F106).
- CONST-wide-addr (COMPLEXITY Z26): an exact number past `i64` is addressed by its sign and the 64-bit limbs of its numerator and denominator in lowest terms, not by its decimal digits, so `canon::VERSION` is 4 and three pinned addresses changed. A power of 2 kept in a `Rat` is built by a shift; a power of an odd prime is still built by num-bigint's squaring, above O(n log n), as before.
- REPLACE-shared-rows (COMPLEXITY F97): the rebuilding walks of a type (`replace_in`, `subst`, `settle`) keep a memo of the rows they made, by address, and `Ck::data` and `vars_outside` a set of the rows they entered. Open, awaiting the author's approval, question A20 (not approved): the program family `p(s, v) = (s, (s, v))` nested n deep is O(n^2), since each call replays its data need on its argument, a type of k rows at the kth call (`Ck::data`), and `bound` and `solved` read the argument's k variables; a memo of the rows `Ck::data` has checked would have to be undone with a trial call (`Ck::undo`). The walks still recurse once per level (T2-ty): the scaling test of the walks uses 100 and 800 levels because 2,000 overflow a 2 MiB stack. Part 3, for question A8: the per-row variable sets cannot all be replaced by the memoized walk. The replacing walks (`holds_any`, `Replacer::holds`) only prune with them and would stay linear in the type without them, given the memo; but `bound`, `solved`, `settled`, `names`, `meet`'s `has_vars`, `hold`'s `facts().holds`, `first_seen`'s pruning, `Sig::settle` and `finished`'s empty test, and `VarFacts::of`'s `free` read them per need, call or signature, so a walk there costs O(size of the type) per query where the set costs O(1) or O(log n): the program family would go from O(n^2) to more. What they read is mostly a yes or no (any class, any name, any bound variable) and the next free name, which a per-row flag and a per-row largest variable number give in O(1) without the sets of F93's disjoint case; `facts().holds(x)` and the variable lists `bound`, `solved` and `settled` need are what remain, and those a memoized walk answers in the size of the type.
- ATOM-nested-sq (COMPLEXITY F106): a payload shape is shared, as a row is, and keeps what it holds at any depth, so atoms nested in atoms are checked in linear time; code that builds a `Shape` calls `Shape::new` and reads its items and fields through `Deref`, and cannot change them in place. With a function n atoms deep, the refusal of `==` still prints O(n^2) characters (2^n for a payload held twice), as F89 (b) and (c) for tuples, open for the author.
- TUPLE-eq-dup (COMPLEXITY F109): `RowData` keeps the kernel type of its tuple shape, made in `Row::made`, replacing the `shape_kernel` walk; the field is a plain `Option<KType>`, since a lazily filled `OnceLock` in a row used as a map key fails clippy's `mutable_key_type`. Rows that hold a variable or the unknown keep none, as before.
- ATOM-payload-open (COMPLEXITY row ATOM-payload-open): a variable, the unknown or a function in an atom's payload inside a tuple is held by the tuple at any depth (`Row::open`, `Row::dynamic`, `Ty::closed`, `Ty::kernel`), so the data need of `==` on a returned row reaches the parameter whose variable sits in a payload. A row's `open` and `dynamic` are read through `Ty::closed` and `Ty::holds_unknown` of each component, which read rows and payload shapes at their outer level.
- TY-subst-stacks (COMPLEXITY row TY-subst-stacks): `subst`, `replace_in`, `settle` and the drop of a type keep their own stack, so a type of any depth is replaced, settled and dropped on a 2 MiB stack; results and visit order are unchanged.
- T2-types-rest-4 (COMPLEXITY K39): the `Debug` text of a type changed where a `Type` is shared, and so did the numbers of diagram nodes and expressions: a type that holds other types (one with an atom, whose payload is a type, or a tuple or morphism part that is not a leaf) and is reached twice or more is written once as `#k=Type(..)` and then as `#k#`, and `k` counts from 0 over the whole text in the order written, where part 3 counted from 0 in each part. Types are told apart by value (the type's digest and comparison), not by address, so equal types built apart get one number and the text stays a function of the type, as zedc's query keys need. Types that hold no other type (`int`, a literal, a recursive type) are written in full each time, as before. The 28 sample texts of `debug_text.txt` have no shared type and are unchanged. `Part` `not` and `zip` run on heap frames, 1.3 to 1.9 times the time of the recursion in a debug build.
- NARROW-hub-settle (COMPLEXITY C26): outside `@exact`, a test whose relation is a difference of two int names (`a >= n`, `n <= a`, `a - n >= 3`, and their negations) is no longer kept as a written constraint; the relation's projection of the difference gives what enumerating it gave (inferred from the spoke's projection being exact; checked by `l01_39_reduced_product::lazy` and the l01_29, l01_38, l01_39, l01_39a and l01_39b groups, all passing unchanged). A written constraint would pin its names, so they would be no leaves of a lazy scope. Equality spokes (`ai == n`) stay eager. Author questions on this item are in its ledger line.
- NARROW-hub-rest (COMPLEXITY C26): outside `@exact`, a test whose relation is a bound on a sum of int names, each of coefficient one or minus one (`a >= n + m`, `a + m >= n`, `a - n - m >= 3`, and their negations), is no longer kept as a written constraint, extending NARROW-hub-settle's rule for a difference of two names; a coefficient of two (`a >= n + n + m`) keeps it. With all names but one fixed, enumerating such a test keeps the last name on one side of a bound, which the relation's projection gives as well (inferred from that; checked by `l01_39_reduced_product::lazy` with three-name constraints and the l01_29, l01_38, l01_39, l01_39a, l01_39b and l01_39c groups, all passing unchanged). The rejection notes of `ai >= n + m` are those of before, which name `m >= -1` and `m >= -2` but not `n >= -1`; the full-derivation rule of L1.29 asks for `n >= -1`, a follow-up item, since this item keeps every note as it was.
- NARROW-hub-related (COMPLEXITY C26): a star of two hubs or more is projected together with the conjuncts among its hubs, so a test among the hubs (`n >= m`, `n + m >= 0`) leaves its leaves lazy and their product exact; without them, `n + m >= 0` would give `a0` int[-1..] where it is int[0..] (the eager-answer test fails so, checked once with the core removed). The lazy-versus-eager random test cannot see this, since both scopes project stars the same way; the eager-answer test does.
- NARROW-notes-name (DESIGN step L1.29): notes may now name fewer tests than before where a carrying test's path gives the read name its product from the other names' products at that test (the earlier tests that moved the name are dropped); the l01_29, l01_38, l01_39 and l01_39a groups pass unchanged, and the tests that count notes are in l01_39a.
- NARROW-eager-scope (DESIGN step L1.29): notes name fewer tests where a carrying test alone gives a name it read the product it carried (`m >= -2` under `m >= -1` is dropped), and name the tests of a longer path where no shortest path explains the carried name; the l01_29, l01_38, l01_39 and l01_39a groups pass with the expected sets updated in l01_39a only
- ROW-eq-deep (COMPLEXITY F118): `Ty`, `Row`, `Shape` and `Sig` compare by `Same`, a comparison on its own stack with Hopcroft and Karp's union-find over the addresses of shared nodes; `Ty`'s `Hash` is written out to match the derived one, since clippy's `derived_hash_with_manual_eq` refuses a derived `Hash` beside a written `PartialEq`. `Sig::facts` is a cache and still takes no part. The needs of a signature are still compared by `==` on each type, one comparison each, as they nest only as deep as a call's text. Addresses are keys only while both operands are borrowed.

(tuple 1 _))) (& (tuple 1 #a1) (! (tuple 1 _))))` and `((0, #a0) | (1, #a1)) \ (1, _)` in both builds, and the case is in `record::printed_types` (COMPLEXITY.md K47).
- After L1.37a, an import's `pin` is compared by address alone: there are no contents to fall back on, since the pinned unit may be absent. Not checked at L1. The weak topological order (`wto`) breaks ties by address and then by form, so its order differs between the two builds; its results do not (`m0_05` passes in both).
- After L1.17a: `Ord` on numbers cannot fail, so after a refused comparison it gives the order of the normal forms until the run stops with the refusal. A sort inside one run that meets both a refused pair and the same pair by value could see an inconsistent order; the run still stops with the refusal, but the sort's result before that is not specified.

- After T14-occ (COMPLEXITY.md K25): `Provenance::origins` reads a family entry by entry when
  no family it was made from, or made from it, has been read, at the cost of the set of
  failures each entry holds, so `n` entries holding one set of `m` failures cost `n·m` (no
  worse than before). Fixed by PROV-family-entries: entries holding one set are counted once,
  and a changed entry is compared shallowly; entries whose sets grow by one failure each are
  still `O(n²)` (K25); fixed by PROV-union-growing. Its oldest-first pass read every fact `i` reaches, so a failure read beside a large value holding none cost that value's parts; fixed by PROV-oldest-failing. Since ADDR-keys (COMPLEXITY.md K40), `deep.rs` and `origins` key shared
  sequences by `Items::id`, not by address.

  and "`positional` parameter after a named one" are set by the AST builder. (c) The
  `for T in …` clause DESIGN decides for printed bounds is not in the `Type` grammar. (d) The
  printed-type check leaves out what type syntax cannot state: families, payloads, the names

  and `counts` are on in any build that includes `zed-tests`, since cargo unifies features in
  a workspace build; a release build of `zedc` alone has both off. A `default-members` list
  without `tests` would fix a plain `cargo build` but not `cargo build --workspace`, which

  the runtime library `libzed_native.a` that `zedc build` links).

## Postmortem amendments (spec commit after 678e2d1)

The postmortem of related languages (array, dataflow, Datalog and incremental languages; report kept outside the repo) proposed nine amendments. Applied to DESIGN.md:
- A3: `@export("c", "symbol")` lets C call pure Zed functions (sec:ffi; tested in L7.4).
- A1: `zed build --explain-plan` (L5.10).
- A2: a lowering stays within a constant factor of `f::cost`; `@cost(depth: …)`.
- A6: recompute or incremental is chosen per wave by cost; the L7 gate adds a 50%-dirty check.
- A7: the L5 ratios are one core against one core, and efficiency is measured against the Rust baseline. L5.14 now agrees with the gate table.

Not applied:
- A4 (name a first domain): a product decision with no effect on what is built.
- A5 (license): the repo already has LICENSE; the choice belongs to the owner.
- A8 (two-process test at L7): distribution is post-v1 in sec:targets.
- A9 (count programs needing generations): no decision depends on the count.

## M0.3 — desugaring rules (done, in the spec)

The spec's sec:grammar now has a `desugar` fence with one rule per syntactic production (107), and the spec repo's `tools/grammar/check.py` checks that rules and productions match one to one. A negative control (an extra rule and a duplicate) is caught. Nothing in this repo changes until the L1 elaborator, which implements these rules. Decisions:
- `&&` and `||` desugar to `if`, not to the strict primitives; the spec calls them selection nodes that short-circuit observationally.
- Recursion enters at L6. A recursive function is the definedness fix over its parameter domain, evaluated on demand. The M0.9 evaluator computes that fix over a finite domain only; the L1/L6 interpreter evaluates on demand.
- Kernel primitives the rules name that the M0.9 kernel lacks: `pow` (L1), `range` (L3), and the type test `is` (L1). They are added at those steps.
- `break e`: the step's guard returns `#break(e)` and the loop's result is `e`.
- T14 and T11 findings are now nodes in `docs/plan_dag.md`, with a table placing each finding
  (section "T14 and T11 findings"): T14-bugs-rt, T14-bugs-kernel, T14-value, T14-heap,
  T14-hooks, T14-rope, T14-bring, T14-paths, T14-provenance, T14-low, T14-rest, T11-preimage,
  T11-ty, T11-check, T11-elab. T14 itself blocks nothing; T12 needs every fix node, and
  L1.41 needs T12 (since 4420380, L1.40 needs only T4, ST1 and ST2). Plan only: no code changed.
- `Prim::TupleGet` on a `fix` under construction is a type error, `TupleGet applied to a function`: the Partial case in `Machine::prim` tests only `Prim::Get`, while `elab.rs` gives `v[0]` as `TupleGet`. No term reaches it today, since `elab.rs` makes no `Term::Fix`; the elaboration of definedness `fix` must write `Get` or the case must take both, as the port case does.
| CLASS-atoms-followups | merged and pushed in batch 65 (e36d9734, with CHECK-else-notfound); no spec change | COMPLEXITY.md F119. Four parts. (a) The key `k` of `m[k]` is now followed (`resolve::Resolver::flow`), so `r(fs, args, k) { d = k else 0; return fs[args[k] else ""]; }` runs and gives `hello\|missing` (CLASS-atoms-index-key, rejected on main: "`r` applies, does not take #_"); with the else fix below, the 6 `given` failures that made CLASS-atoms leave the key out no longer occur. (b) `f(positional x) = g(x)` with no handler in `f` takes no atom, kept as written, decided by standing rules, no question asked: DESIGN sec:type_resolution counts the loose atoms that reach "an `else`, `is`, `==` or `!=` in the body that handles it", and says "an inferred parameter and the declared parameter that holds the same atoms give the same results"; the declared twin `f(x: String)` passes `#nf` by at `f(#nf)`, as the author's Q1 (bubbling) gives, so the inferred one does too. Pinned by `handled::passing_the_parameter_to_a_function_that_handles_its_atoms_handles_none` (`g(#nf)` gives `"dflt"`, `f(#nf)` and a lambda form give `#nf`). (c) `p == #a` and `p != #a` now narrow `p` in each branch as `p is #a` does (`Ck::atom_eq`, from `Frame::CondOf`): `if x == #nf {"dflt"} else {trim(x)}` is `(String \| #nf) -> String`, declared or inferred, either operand order, and `!=` (eqa, eqb2 now give `dflt`). A path operand (`p[0] == #a`, tup2b2) is not narrowed, as `is` on a path is not: open, L2.6b struct positions. (d) lita, `x == 3`, handles no atom and is correct as is: "a plain literal compared by `==` with a class narrows it as an operand does", so `x` is `int`, `f(#nf)` gives `#nf` by rule 6; pinned by `handled::an_equality_with_a_number_handles_no_atom`. Mechanism: `narrowed` on a class that may hold other values no longer leaves the tested class itself; it gives a class of its own linked to it (`Class::narrowed_to`, `Class::narrows` so that a test of a test's value follows the chain), and `join_narrowed` at the function's end, before its signature, merges each link where either end lacks other values; a merge that fails is an error at the test: "the value this test leaves is used as int, and the value it tests as String" (`given::a_value_left_by_a_test_used_against_the_tested_value_is_rejected_at_the_test`). Tests: `handled` +3, `given` +3, `atom_scaling::chains_of_tests_that_leave_values_of_their_own_scale` (F119), `signatures::a_parameter_any_value_fits_gets_a_type_variable` changed (`r else 0` no longer gives the parameter's own variable). Red before green: on the branch's base sources 6 of l01_26 fail: 5 of the 7 new tests and the changed one (the pins of (b) and (d) pass there, as they should). Affected groups pass: l01_26 290 (1 ignored), l01_16, l01_27, l01_39, zedc-check, zedc-resolve (54 pieces); all `^l0\|^zedc` groups 655 pieces, 2 failed, both comparing against the spec checkout and not touching the checker (`l01_08_parser::cases::the_copies_match_the_spec`: zed/grammar out of date; `l02_03_name_resolution::later_words`: the word `failure` missing from the table). Open: k1/k5 "does not take (#_)" (also on main); k7, atoms passed at an outer call bypass the body's `else` (over-approximation, sound); `strip_widened` intersects across needs shared by memo (conservative). |
| CHECK-else-notfound | merged and pushed in batch 65 (e36d9734, with CLASS-atoms-followups); no spec change | QUESTIONS.md A11's side observation (`g(positional r) = r else 0` made `"${g(args[1])}"` of type `0 \| String \| #notFound`, "whether that is the bubbling rule or a checker fault was not traced"): traced, a checker fault. `narrowed` on a parameter of any value (a type variable, a class holding other values) left the class itself, so `r else 0` was `r`'s own variable and a call gave the argument whole, atoms included; DESIGN sec:failure strips the loose atoms an `else` handles. Now the `else` leaves a class of its own (CLASS-atoms-followups above), `handled_sig` gives one arrow with the handled loose atoms dropped from the result (`Ck::widened_other`, `strip_widened`, the needs stripped in `generalize`), and `g(args[1])` is `0 \| String`. Tests: `given::an_else_on_a_parameter_of_any_value_leaves_no_atom_at_a_call` (three programs run at a `Lines` port: `0`, `zeroa`, and `{ d = r else 0; return r; }` giving `abc`). A11's own program, `"${g(args[1])}" ++ "a"` with `r else 0`, is now rejected by `++` ("does not take (0, \"a\")"), which is right for `0 \| String`. |
